Captured 2026-08-04 from conversation, deliberately at "capture the
idea" depth, not fully designed yet:

Whenever the user says to modify some feature (in the org skill's
scope), first check whether the request matches any existing TODO
heading, including =DONE= ones:
- If it matches an existing heading, ask the user for clarification
  (e.g. "this looks like it overlaps with <heading> — same task, or
  something new?") rather than silently creating a duplicate or
  silently assuming it's the same thing.
- If it doesn't match anything, create a new heading, capture it (via
  =org_capture=), set it to =PLANNING=, ask the user any necessary
  clarifying questions, and then offer to plan it properly (i.e. enter
  Plan Mode). If the user declines planning, revert the heading back
  to =TODO= for later planning rather than leaving it stuck in
  =PLANNING=.

Separately, also check whether the proposed change interacts with any
*open* (non-DONE, non-CANCELLED) task via a depends-on relationship in
either direction — i.e. either the new change depends on unfinished
work, or some existing open task depends on the thing being changed —
and surface that to the user.

Open questions deferred to actual planning, not resolved here: how
"matches an existing TODO" is actually determined (semantic search via
=org_query=? something else?), whether discovering an existing
=:BLOCKER:= depends-on link (this heading's own, above) is sufficient
or whether the check also needs to search prose for informal
cross-references that predate this convention, and exactly when in the
org skill's flow this check should fire.

** MAYBE Make org-blocker-hook actually consult :BLOCKER: :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       087e73e9-bb24-43b6-a972-b7f103d04017
:CREATED:  [2026-08-04 Tue 17:46]
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/blocker-hook-enforcement.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-04, surfaced while adding a =:BLOCKER:= property to
the sibling heading above: this project's own
=claude-code-ide-org--blocker-clock-running-p= (the one function
currently on =org-blocker-hook=) only denies a =DONE= transition while
a clock is running on that same heading — it never reads =:BLOCKER:=
at all, and org-depend itself isn't loaded anywhere in this project
(=packages.el= notes no additional packages). So every existing
=:BLOCKER:= property in =TODO.org=/=DONE.org= (e.g. ID
799b14be-6849-4e22-8a76-4e214d8a25fe's) is purely advisory today —
=org_set_todo= will happily transition a blocked heading to =DONE=
with nothing stopping it, contrary to what the property visually
implies.

*Premise falsified 2026-08-13: org-depend IS loaded and IS on the hook.*
Measured in the live Emacs, =org-blocker-hook= holds
=(org-block-todo-from-children-or-siblings-or-parent
org-depend-block-todo claude-code-ide-org--blocker-clock-running-p)=,
and =org-enforce-todo-dependencies= is =t=. The inference above went
from "=packages.el= adds no packages" to "org-depend isn't loaded",
which is wrong: Doom loads it as part of org, so nothing had to be
added.

Corroborating measurement rather than inference:
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]], =TODO= with three
unfinished blockers, reports =(org-entry-blocked-p)= = *YES*. Something
is evaluating =:BLOCKER:=, and this project's own function does not.

*What is NOT established, deliberately left open rather than assumed.*
A scratch reproduction — two headings in a temp file, one blocking the
other — did *not* reproduce it: neither reported blocked and both
accepted =DONE=. That is far more likely a fault in the test's id
resolution than a contradiction, but it means the end-to-end claim
"=org-todo= to =DONE= is actually denied" is *unproven*. So this heading
is not cancelled: its premise is dead, its goal may or may not already
be met, and settling that needs a reproduction that works.

*A second finding, which the heading would want either way:* blocking is
evaluated against the *blocked heading's own state*.
[[id:0c8644ff-f7b1-4789-ac1e-babf2e9fbc8e][0c8644ff]] is =MAYBE= with an
unfinished blocker and reports blocked=no, while =02aaae22= at =TODO=
reports YES. So a =:BLOCKER:= on a =MAYBE= heading is dormant — it looks
enforcing and is not, until the heading leaves =MAYBE=.

Extend =claude-code-ide-org--blocker-clock-running-p= (or add a second
=org-blocker-hook= function alongside it) to also deny a transition to
=DONE= while any ID listed in the heading's own =:BLOCKER:= property
resolves to a heading that isn't itself in a done state (=DONE= or
=CANCELLED=). Should reuse =org-id-find= the same way the existing
clock-marker check and =org_set_todo='s post-transition heading
lookup already do. Whether to also honor the inverse =:TRIGGER:=
property (documented in the org skill alongside =:BLOCKER:=) is an
open question for planning — =org-depend= itself implements both, but
this project may only need one direction enforced.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/capture-commit-safety.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-04 from conversation: right now, capturing a new org
heading (per CLAUDE.md's "any time a new task is described in
conversation, create an org heading" rule) only edits the working tree
— nothing commits it, so it sits as an uncommitted change alongside
whatever else is in progress until a human notices and commits by
hand. Tell the org skill to commit the capture itself, immediately,
*given that it's safe to do so*.

"Safe" needs pinning down during planning, but at minimum: only the
=.org= file(s) touched by the capture are staged (never a broad
=git add -A=/=git add .=, per this project's own general git-safety
practice), the repo isn't mid-rebase/mid-merge/otherwise in a weird
state, and — the tricky part — the capture commit doesn't get tangled
up with *other* unrelated uncommitted changes already sitting in the
same file from earlier in the session (a commit should be scoped to
the capture, not sweep up unrelated in-progress edits just because
they're in the same file). Whether that means a targeted
=git commit -- TODO.org= (commits the whole file's current diff,
including any unrelated hunks) is actually "safe" in the sense meant
here, or whether something narrower is needed, is exactly the kind of
question actual planning should resolve rather than deciding here.

Should this be prose guidance in the skill (the model decides "is this
one safe" case by case, same register as the existing read-only-buffer
guidance) or something more mechanical? Lean prose, given the "safe"
judgment call looks inherently contextual rather than a fixed
checklist — same reasoning CLAUDE.md already uses elsewhere for calls
that "only the model can make."

*Update 2026-08-05:* substantial overlap with
[[id:782cda6c-111c-454e-93eb-b2b27012078b][Audit for clock/session bookkeeping noise before committing during a clocking-suppressed session]]
— that heading is a concrete, evidence-backed version of the exact
open question above ("doesn't get tangled up with other unrelated
uncommitted changes"). Not merging them here; worth deciding during
planning whether they should be one task, sequenced, or just
cross-referenced as they are now.

-----

*Scope widened 2026-08-10, and the heading retitled to match.* What
started as "is =PLANNING= pulling its weight" became a review of the
whole progression, after checking the sequence against org+GTD practice
on the web. The original question is a sub-case and is answered in part
below.

*Census of TODO.org the same day, which is the most useful input here:*

: 23 MAYBE   14 TODO   14 DONE   2 NEXT   2 DOING   1 CANCELLED
:  0 PLANNING   0 WAIT

Those 56 are the *keyworded* headings; the file holds 63, the
difference being the seven top-level category headings, which carry no
TODO keyword by convention.

*What the tradition says — both findings cut against having =DOING=:*
- The canonical org+GTD reference (Bernt Hansen, https://doc.norang.ca/org-mode.html)
  runs =TODO(t) NEXT(n) | DONE(d!)= plus =WAITING(w@/!) HOLD(h@/!) |
  CANCELLED(c@)=. No in-progress keyword at all: *clocking* signals what
  is active, and he goes as far as switching tasks *to* =NEXT= on
  clock-in. =NEXT= means "available to work on now."
- GTD proper treats an in-progress state as a smell. A next action is
  "the next physical, visible activity"; anything needing an
  in-progress marker is usually a project wearing an action's clothes,
  and the prescribed fix is decomposition, not another keyword.

*=DOING= survives that critique here, for the reason it usually fails
it.* The standard substitute — let the clock say what is active — is
exactly the affordance
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] removes. CLAUDE.md
already commits to clock accuracy being "only ever as fresh as the last
time a human ran the review pass, not live." A reviewed historical
record cannot answer "what is being worked on right now," so the
keyword has to. Worth recording explicitly, because a future reader
citing Bernt or GTD would have a well-supported argument for deleting
=DOING= and would be wrong *for this system specifically*.

The user's own narrowing helps too: =DOING= means actively working on
implementation and testing — a named phase, not an open-ended
in-progress limbo, which is what GTD actually objects to.

*Proposal 1, the substantive one: add =VERIFY= between =DOING= and
=DONE=.* Headings here repeatedly sit in =DOING= carrying "*Still
owed:* an interactive by-hand pass" — implementation and tests
complete, verification outstanding. Under the definition above that is
not =DOING=, and under this project's "verified, not just implemented"
bar it is certainly not =DONE=. The state is *structural*, not
incidental: verification is human-triggered by hard constraint (Claude
cannot run the interactive apply), so every such task passes through a
mandatory handoff that currently has no name and lives only as prose.
org's own manual uses exactly this slot in its example sequence,
=#+TODO: TODO FEEDBACK VERIFY | DONE CANCELED= (org-manual.org:4210).

The payoff is a real queue: =VERIFY= becomes "what is waiting on *me*
to confirm," which is a next-action list in the GTD sense and does not
exist today in any queryable form.

: #+TODO: TODO NEXT PLANNING DOING VERIFY WAIT MAYBE | DONE CANCELLED

Clock rule if adopted: =DOING= → =VERIFY= closes the clock, since Claude
has stopped; the human's verification time clocks against the same
heading, which the convention recorded at
[[id:a1d63f52-4c07-4b9e-8f31-6d2b90c4e7a5][a1d63f52]] already permits.

*Proposal 2: get =MAYBE= out of the default agenda without changing the
keyword.* At 23 of the file's 56 keyworded headings it is the largest —
a perfectly healthy Someday/Maybe list, except that sitting before the
=|= renders it actionable. GTD is emphatic that Someday/Maybe lives
outside the active system and is reviewed periodically. Bernt's answer
is to keep such states active but filter them from default agenda views;
moving =MAYBE= after the =|= would be semantically wrong, since these
are not finished.

*Proposal 3: narrow =WAIT= to "waiting on a person."* It has never
been used once. GTD separates *Waiting For* (delegated, needs follow-up)
from *blocked* (dependency), and dependencies here are already expressed
structurally via =:BLOCKER:=. The current gloss, "blocked or waiting on
someone," overlaps that — and a keyword nobody reaches for is usually
one whose meaning is unclear rather than one whose situation never
arises.

*Bearing on this heading's original question.* The tradition supports a
planning state on its merits: planning is first-class GTD activity (the
Natural Planning Model), and =PLANNING= is on that basis *better*
grounded than =DOING=. But that answers only half the complaint recorded
above. Whether the *machinery* earns its complexity — the
=ExitPlanMode=-driven auto-promotion, the single global
=--planning-owner-session-id=, the hand-reversions logged above — is a
separate question the tradition says nothing about, and it stays open.
Do not read "GTD likes planning states" as "keep the hook."

Still =MAYBE=, still deferred on the original terms. Nothing above is
adopted; the sequence is unchanged pending a decision.

*A second question arrived 2026-08-14, and it belongs here rather than in
a heading of its own: should the set gain a =REVIEW= keyword?*

It came out of a live problem. =WAIT= had quietly acquired a second
meaning — the resting state at the end of a Claude work increment,
meaning "the ball is in the human's court" — used twice on 2026-08-13
before anyone noticed it was a pattern (see
[[id:d1cf852a-696a-4195-a4a7-039439c1f4b7][d1cf852a]]). Asked whether
=TODO= would serve the same end and be more idiomatic, the answer turned
out to be neither: *the keyword field is one slot serving two actors with
different views of who owes what.* =WAIT= is coherent from the session's
side (I handed this to someone else and am blocked on them — which is
=WAITING='s canonical GTD meaning) and odd from the human's, who does not
wait on themselves. =TODO= is coherent from the human's side and discards
both "work has happened here" and "this is owed back to you".

=REVIEW= is the only candidate that reads correctly from *both* sides,
and it does three things a rename would not:

- *It de-overloads =WAIT=,* which goes back to meaning blocked on an
  external party — someone you may have to chase. Those two states call
  for opposite actions and are currently indistinguishable.
- *It is a stage, not a parking state.* =WAIT= and =MAYBE= are places
  work sits; =REVIEW= is a step work passes through, between =DOING= and
  =DONE=. That gives it a principled position in the sequence.
- *It makes the session-start report actionable.* That report already
  surfaces every =WAIT= heading; "3 awaiting your review" is a list to
  act on, "3 WAIT" is not, and the two could then be reported apart.

*The objection to weigh against it is vocabulary collision.* The project
would then carry three meanings of "review": the =:review:= tag (a kind
of work), =claude-code-ide-org-review= (the command that applies the
queue), and a state. "The review buffer shows a REVIEW item tagged
:review:" is a sentence someone would have to write. Either choose a
different word for the state or adopt the collision deliberately and say
so in CLAUDE.md.

*Why it lands here rather than in its own heading:* adding a keyword
while this heading asks whether an existing one earns its keep should be
one decision about the *set*, not two taken months apart. The same
argument recorded above — that the assessment wants accumulated real use
rather than spot impressions — applies to =REVIEW= unchanged.

*Every place the keyword set is written down,* inventoried 2026-08-14 by
grep rather than from memory, because the cost is spread wider than it
looks and a half-applied keyword fails in confusing ways. Line numbers
are as of that date; the names are the durable part.

*Must change to add a keyword:*

- =TODO.org= line 1 — =#+TODO:= with per-keyword logging cookies
  (=TODO(t!) NEXT(n!) PLANNING(p!) DOING(d!) WAIT(w@/!) MAYBE(m!) |
  DONE(D!) CANCELLED(c@)=). A new keyword needs its cookie chosen too,
  and =REVIEW= collides with =CANCELLED= on =c= and with nothing on =R=.
- =clock-template.org= line 2 — the same directive *without* cookies, so
  the two files already disagree about logging.
- =~/.config/doom/config.el= =org-todo-keywords= (line 65) — the global
  default, which is what files carrying no =#+TODO:= line inherit.
- =CLAUDE.md= — *two* tables: "TODO keyword semantics" and "State
  transition rules". The second needs two new rows, since =DOING ->
  REVIEW= must close the clock and =REVIEW -> DOING= must reopen it.
- =config.el= =org_set_todo='s MCP registration — the valid states are
  enumerated verbatim *twice*, in the tool description and again in the
  =state= argument description (lines 4694, 4708).
- =config.el= =claude-code-ide-org--review-suggest-heading= (line 3097) —
  the list of keywords whose arrival means a heading stops being the
  active one. See [[id:d1cf852a-696a-4195-a4a7-039439c1f4b7][d1cf852a]]:
  this is the span-attribution bracket, and it is a denylist of
  everything except =DOING=/=PLANNING=. Inverting it to test for those
  two would make it immune to this whole question.
- =config.el= =claude-code-ide-org--attention-headings-context= (line
  609; renamed from =--wait-headings-context= by
  [[id:9d7531f5-11c5-4203-89e3-56c3fe399df5][9d7531f5]] on 2026-08-14,
  when it stopped reporting only =WAIT=) — the session-start report now
  matches =WAIT= and =DOING= as two literal string comparisons in a
  =cond=. A =REVIEW= state that neither branch knows about is invisible
  at session start, which is most of the reason to want it. Note the
  =DOING= branch is the one a =REVIEW= keyword would most likely
  *replace*, not join.
- =.claude/skills/org/SKILL.md= — the keyword list at line 43, the
  per-keyword sections (=WAIT= at 36) and the transition table (238-240).
  *Note this list is already stale:* it reads
  =TODO NEXT DOING WAIT MAYBE | DONE CANCELLED=, with =PLANNING= missing
  — which is itself evidence for what this heading is asking.
- [[id:3bd3402b-3ab6-4ace-b20b-3ff84caa3fb2][3bd3402b]]'s lint, once it
  exists, since it will assert against the convention.

*Checked and deliberately unchanged:*

- =config.el= =claude-code-ide-org--outline-finished-keywords= (line
  1627) and the two =("DONE" "CANCELLED")= tests in the assignment
  candidate list (4452, 4462). =REVIEW= is an *active* state, so it
  belongs in none of them — but a future reader will find them while
  grepping and should know they were considered.

*The trap worth naming.* =org_set_todo= validates against
=org-todo-keywords-1=, which is *buffer-local* and derived from the
file's own =#+TODO:= line when it has one. So a keyword added to the Doom
config but not to =TODO.org='s header is accepted in =DONE.org= (no
=#+TODO:= line, inherits the global) and rejected in =TODO.org= with
"not a TODO keyword in this file". Same for
=claude-code-ide-org--pending-capture-keywords=, which reads the target
file's keywords for a capture that has not been written yet. Update the
file headers and the Doom config in the same commit.

*Sequencing.* The change touches =config.el= in four places, and as of
2026-08-14 three unmerged feature branches all carry divergent
=config.el=. Doing this before those are reconciled guarantees conflicts
in a file where a conflict is expensive to resolve correctly.


** TODO Fall back to the queue when org_capture cannot reach Emacs :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       7fa68d5c-b6c0-43df-85c6-764b1d56511f
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 07:31]
:END:

=org_capture= requires a live Emacs. When the server is unreachable the
call fails outright and the task exists only in the conversation, which
ends. Not hypothetical: on 2026-08-10 the Emacs server was down for the
first ten minutes or so of a session (=emacsclient= reporting "can't
find socket"), and any capture attempted then would simply have been
lost.

This is the one creation path that cannot use the property the event
queue exists for — durable, cheap, no Emacs required. Everything else a
session records survives an Emacs outage; the act of writing down a new
task does not.

*Proposal:* attempt the live capture; on failure, append a =capture=
event to the queue, and let apply create the heading.

*Three things this raises, none of them settled:*
- *It is the queue's first outbox use.* Every existing kind is a
  proposal about a heading that already exists; a =capture= event would
  bring one into being. That is a genuinely different contract, and the
  review buffer would need to render it as such.
- *The :ID: must be minted by the session, not at apply time.* An ID
  assigned on application is useless to the session that queued it — it
  could not link to the heading, block on it, or clock against it, which
  is exactly what =claude-code-ide-org-capture= returns its ID for
  today. Minting without Emacs is fine (=org-id-new= is a UUID), but it
  means an ID can be referenced before the heading it names exists.
- *Reconciliation.* If the live capture succeeded and only its reply was
  lost, applying the queued event would duplicate the heading. A
  session-minted ID gives apply a cheap idempotency key: if that :ID:
  already resolves, the event is already realised.

*The larger caveat, which bears on this and on
[[id:cdcc065c-628d-4350-b1ba-fcf686e9c4c1][cdcc065c]] both.* This tool creates a bare
=* TODO <title>= at top level in a single target file. It cannot place a
heading under a named parent, nor carry tags, a body, or any property
beyond the =:ID:= it generates. Consequently almost every heading in
this project is written by direct Elisp instead — four were created that
way on 2026-08-10 alone, none through the tool. So instrumenting or
repairing =org_capture= improves a path that is rarely taken, and the
prior question is whether the tool should become capable enough to be
worth using. Decide that first; it may reorder or absorb both headings.

*Considered and set aside:* queueing a =capture= event on *every*
capture, as an audit record carrying =session_id=/=agent_id= — which is
the only place session attribution exists, and would answer "which
session created this heading?" structurally rather than from prose in
the body. Rejected for now on the caveat above: it would record almost
nothing real. Revisit if the tool becomes the normal creation path.


*Also the timeline origin:* recording creation as a =:LOGBOOK:= entry
([[id:d9132c57-9dcf-47e9-ba74-7f5a1ced3ae5][d9132c57]]) can ride this same event rather
than needing a mechanism of its own — apply would write the creation
entry exactly as it writes a state transition.

** TODO org_capture's no-target fallback writes a level-1 heading, violating the category convention
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       97696fc2-f07b-4586-b280-de6a7d26fb87
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 21:25]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-11 Tue 21:26] \\
  fallback placement defect found by using the tool
:END:

[[id:47c027d2-7b5a-4de0-baf4-f992f3b814bd][47c027d2]] gave =org_capture=
an optional =target= and kept =(file <spec>)= as the fallback when it is
omitted — reasoned at the time as "the honest answer for placement
unknown," which append-at-end-of-file is, in general.

It is wrong for the file capture now points at. Appending to TODO.org
means a *level-1* heading, and this project reserves those for
categories: pure structure, no keyword, no tags, no properties, per
CLAUDE.md's "Top-level headings" rule. So every no-target capture would
produce a heading violating the convention on three counts at once, to
be refiled by hand — the manual step the tool exists to remove.

Nothing has landed wrong yet: both real captures named a target. Found
by using the tool rather than by review.

*Options, none chosen:*
- *Require =target=.* Simplest and most honest — refuse rather than
  guess. Costs the one case the fallback was for: a passing mention
  whose right home genuinely is not known.
- *Fall back to a designated category* rather than the file. Keeps
  capture always-succeeds, at the price of a setting to choose and an
  inbox-shaped thing arriving through the back door, which
  =47c027d2='s discussion rejected at the front.
- *Make the fallback file-aware*: append at top level only where that is
  meaningful, require a target where the file has the category
  convention. Most correct, least obvious, and needs a way to know which
  files have the convention.

Worth deciding alongside
[[id:bd482c92-a71a-4d7d-88df-47678ee94c79][bd482c92]], since this only
bites once the capture target is this project's own file — which is
exactly what that heading makes permanent.

*Second, smaller defect seen in the same capture:* the entry is inserted
with no blank line separating it from the preceding body text or the
following heading, unlike every hand-written heading in this file. Fixed
by hand here. Probably wants =:empty-lines-before 1= on the template.

** TODO org_outline's active_only orphans children of finished headings :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       98908aff-63cc-4b71-b90b-817da361a23b
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 16:06]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-12 Wed 16:07] \\
  found while documenting the tool's own response format
:END:

Indentation is the *only* thing =org_outline='s output says about
parentage: =claude-code-ide-org--outline-line= emits two spaces per
absolute =org-current-level=, and =org-map-entries= emits in document
order. A reader reconstructs the tree as "parent = nearest preceding
line with a smaller indent," which holds only while every intermediate
ancestor is present.

=active_only= breaks that. The filter lives in =--outline-line=, which
returns nil for a DONE/CANCELLED heading — it does *not* skip the
subtree in =--outline-map=, so that heading's still-active descendants
are emitted anyway, at their unchanged absolute indent. Verified
2026-08-12 against a scratch file:

: * Category
: ** DONE Finished parent
: *** TODO Active child

returns

: Category
:     TODO Active child

The indent jumps two levels, and a reader re-parents the child onto its
*grandparent* with nothing in the output marking that it did. Silent
wrong structure, not a visible gap.

*Not currently observable in this project's own files*, which is why it
took this long to notice: TODO.org's level-1 headings are categories
carrying no TODO keyword, so nothing on the path from a category to a
level-2 task can ever be filtered out. All 60 lines of a full-depth
=active_only= run were checked and no indent jump occurs. That is a
property of the top-level-heading convention, not a guarantee from the
format — the bug is one three-level task tree away from biting.

=max_depth= is safe by contrast: it only ever drops the deepest lines,
truncating leaves rather than severing an ancestor.

*Options, none chosen:*
- *Emit the elided ancestors anyway*, unfiltered, so the path stays
  intact. Keeps reconstruction sound and costs a few lines the caller
  asked not to see.
- *Re-indent survivors to their visible depth*, so indent means "depth
  in the returned tree" rather than absolute level. Cheapest output,
  but contradicts the deliberate absolute-level decision in
  [[id:9f3c35c7-0229-4cd6-908b-779267ec9b97][9f3c35c7]] that makes
  =max_depth= mean the same thing for a file and for a subtree.
- *Skip the whole subtree* when a heading is filtered out. Simplest
  rule, and the most likely to surprise — an active child of a DONE
  parent is exactly the thing =active_only= should be showing.

*Related, same output, separate defect:* in a multi-file call the
per-file label is a bare =file-name-nondirectory= at column 0,
structurally identical to a level-1 heading, which by this project's
convention also carries no keyword, no =:ID:= and no tags. Nothing but
"it looks like a filename" distinguishes them, so a reader that treats
the label as a heading turns each file into one spurious root. Worth
fixing in the same pass, since both are the same class of bug: the
format asserting less structure than the reader needs.
** TODO Add a tool to set heading properties, starting with :BLOCKER: :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       36b811d9-8bd3-4a32-b67d-39ccfe0d66c1
:CREATED:  [2026-08-15 Sat 16:43]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-15 Sat 16:44] \\
  property tool gap, three incidents tonight
:END:

None of the 14 registered tools sets a property. =org_capture= writes
=:ID:= and =:CREATED:= at creation and that is the whole of it, so every
later property change is a hand-edit or an =emacsclient= call.

*The gap has a behavioural cost, not just an ergonomic one.* CLAUDE.md's
convention says to prefer =:BLOCKER:= over a prose "depends on ..."
sentence, because a property is machine-checkable and a sentence is not.
But there is no tool to write one, while prose costs nothing — so the
*discouraged* form is cheaper than the *preferred* one, and the
convention loses by default. Evidence from the session that found this:
on 2026-08-15 a dependency was recorded on
[[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]] as the words
"Tracked separately", with no link and no property, and only got a real
=:BLOCKER:= when the omission was noticed on re-reading. Twice in the
same session a property had to be set through =emacsclient= —
=org-entry-put= on that heading and on
[[id:9ae4b17e-278c-482f-9114-df28b2254806][9ae4b17e]].

*Validation is the reason to build the specific tool rather than only a
general one.* A =:BLOCKER:= naming an ID that does not exist never blocks
anything and never errors — it simply does nothing, forever. A tool that
resolves each ID before writing turns that silent failure into a refusal
at the call site. The same session fabricated a UUID from an 8-character
prefix and caught it only by a link-resolution sweep afterwards, which is
precisely the failure this validation would have made impossible.

*Design questions, unresolved:*

- *General =org_set_property= or specific =org_add_blocker=?* The general
  one is obviously more flexible and validates nothing. The specific one
  encodes the convention, can resolve IDs, and can append to an existing
  =ids(...)= list rather than overwriting it — which the general form
  would silently get wrong, since =:BLOCKER:= holds a *set*.
- *Write immediately or queue?* A property is neither TODO state nor
  clock, so it is outside what the queue exists to defer. =org_amend='s
  model looks right: write through Emacs when the file is free, queue when
  the human has unsaved changes in that buffer.
- *What else needs setting?* =:ARCHIVE:= per-heading overrides and
  =:TRIGGER:= (the inverse of =:BLOCKER:=) are the other two the
  conventions name. =:CREATED:= and =:ID:= are already handled at capture
  and should stay there.

Distinct from [[id:087e73e9-bb24-43b6-a972-b7f103d04017][087e73e9]],
which is about *enforcing* =:BLOCKER:= once set. This one is about being
able to set it at all.

*Footnote to the above, written the same minute:* the paragraph arguing
that a fabricated =:BLOCKER:= ID fails silently was itself written with a
fabricated ID — =8ee85ba3-...=, invented for this very link, caught by
the resolve-check rather than by reading it back. Third occurrence in one
session. The lesson is not "be careful"; it is that the resolve-check is
the only thing that has ever caught this, and a tool that validates at the
call site would remove the opportunity entirely.

*A fourth design constraint, found by =bin/lint-org= within a minute of
this heading being written.* Setting =:BLOCKER:= on
[[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]] naming
[[id:df5afbbd-0eac-4032-be91-17cc22ef0cd7][df5afbbd]] produced:

: error: :BLOCKER: names keyword-less heading df5afbbd -- org-depend
: blocks only on an unfinished TODO, so this is a silent no-op

The linter is right, and the cause is architectural rather than careless.
=org_capture= deliberately writes no TODO keyword — its own description
says to set the state afterwards with =org_set_todo= — and =org_set_todo=
queues. So *every heading captured in a session is keyword-less until a
human applies the queue*, and any =:BLOCKER:= naming one is inert in the
meantime. Not wrong, but not yet in force, and indistinguishable from
working without the linter.

This sharpens the validation argument above. A property tool should
refuse, or at least warn, when the heading it is about to name carries no
keyword — the same check the linter already implements, moved from
after-the-fact to the call site. It also suggests a smaller companion
question: whether =org_capture= should accept an initial state, so that a
capture and its keyword do not arrive a review pass apart.

Worth noting the linter caught this and re-reading did not, which is the
same lesson as the fabricated ID above and the same remedy: mechanical
checks catch what looks correct.
* Clock lifecycle & visibility
:PROPERTIES:
:ARCHIVE:  DONE.org::* Clock lifecycle & visibility
:END:

Everything here is specifically about the clock subsystem's
correctness and observability, as opposed to the general TODO-state
machine above — three different angles on "make sure the clock is
never silently wrong."

** TODO Direct file writes to a live org buffer can desync org-clock-marker :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a
:CREATED:  [2026-08-04 Tue 18:27]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from "MAYBE"      [2026-08-14 Fri 13:51] \\
  confirmed surviving; three dependents
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/clock-marker-desync-guard.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-04, hit live during this session's TODO.org commit-
splitting work: writing TODO.org's content directly via =git
hash-object=/=update-index= (bypassing Emacs) and relying on
=global-auto-revert-mode= to pick up the change desynced Emacs's
*in-memory* clock state from the file text. =org-clock-marker= kept
pointing at its old raw character offset, which after the rewrite
landed on an unrelated =:SESSIONS:= line instead of the =CLOCK:= line
— =org_clock_out= then failed outright with "Clock start time is
gone", and the statusline (which reads live =org-clocking-p=/
=org-clock-marker= state, not the file text — see
=claude-code-ide-org--statusline-task-string=) kept showing "clocked
in" even after a text-level recovery via
=claude-code-ide-org-close-open-interval= had already closed the
=CLOCK:= line on disk.

Worked around manually this time: reset
=org-clock-marker=/=org-clock-hd-marker=/=org-clock-start-time=
directly via =emacsclient=, then called
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-status-hook-out= by hand since normal
=org-clock-out= never got far enough to fire it. The existing "Stale
interval recovery" design (CLAUDE.md) only repairs the *text* side of
a stale interval (crash/shutdown case, where Emacs's own in-memory
state is simply gone after a restart) — it has no equivalent for *this*
case, where Emacs is still running but its live clock state has gone
stale relative to a file it didn't itself edit. Whether the real fix
is "never write live org files outside Emacs, full stop" (a
process/discipline fix, not code) or something more automatic (e.g.
detecting the marker/text mismatch and self-healing) is a question for
planning.

*Second, distinct incident, 2026-08-05*: on
[[id:4cda6bf7-441b-47b2-b3d5-b8c7e7a207a5][Show the TODO keyword, not just clocked in/out, in the statusline]],
after a cross-session clock resume (see the =d150c02e= sibling below)
followed by a proper =org_clock_out=, the =:LOGBOOK:= briefly
contained a genuinely malformed line — bare =CLOCK:= with *no*
timestamp at all — sitting between two valid entries, before
=claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= rebuilt the drawer and it
disappeared (the arithmetic of what remained checks out, so nothing
was silently lost, but *why* a timestamp-less =CLOCK:= line got
written in the first place is unconfirmed — a race between two
near-simultaneous writers to the same drawer is the working
hypothesis, not proven). A second, structurally different way the
same "shared mutable file" pressure can corrupt =:LOGBOOK:=, worth
folding into whatever planning addresses this cluster.

Also worth noting for whoever plans =4cda6bf7='s own fix: swapping
=status-label= for =org-get-todo-state= *at the clock marker* is
still marker-dependent — it inherits this exact desync mode (a stale
TODO keyword shown instead of a stale in/out label), it doesn't
route around it.

*Kept and narrowed 2026-08-13, by the user's decision.* Reviewed against
its two partial owners rather than as keep-or-cancel, because that is
what it actually is: a gap between them.

*What is retired.* The original =org_clock_out= failure is unreachable —
that function opens and closes nothing since
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]]'s cutover. So is
the concurrent-writer =CLOCK:= corruption:
=claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= has no production caller left,
only tests.

*What survives, and is the whole of this heading now.* Two surfaces,
each only partly owned elsewhere:
1. =--trigger-auto-clock-in= (config.el:1778, registered :1941) still
   opens a real clock on *any* transition to =DOING=/=PLANNING=,
   including a human's own =C-c C-t=. Apply suppresses it via the
   re-entrancy guard; a hand-edit does not.
   [[id:ab75d6d2-0e59-405d-92c6-2c67488db133][ab75d6d2]] covers
   *container* headings only, so leaf hand-edits remain.
2. =--statusline-task-string= (config.el:659) still reads
   =org-clocking-p=/=org-clock-marker=/=org-clock-history= directly.
   [[id:290b6fc5-3ebb-4827-9a97-5a3542e354b6][290b6fc5]] is scoped to
   the statusline's *data source*, not to marker desync generally.

Cancel this and the uncovered middle belongs to nobody. The deciding
fact against cancelling is that the desync was observed *live on
2026-08-12*, after the cutover, and recorded on =ab75d6d2= — not a
theoretical residue.

*Newly established 2026-08-13, and it raises this heading's stakes.*
Reading =org-clock.el= for
[[id:803314aa-eda9-4d48-9e43-b3eb4802a4e8][803314aa]] found that
=org-clock-out= signals =(error "Clock start time is gone")=
(org-clock.el:1841) exactly when =org-clock-marker='s line is no longer
a =CLOCK:= line — i.e. precisely this heading's desync. That is the same
error incident 1 above recorded. So this desync is not merely a display
problem: it is the reachable trigger for apply's fabricated-interval
bug. The two headings are one causal chain, and this one is upstream.

*Confirmed surviving, and promoted out of =MAYBE=, 2026-08-14 by
[[id:e51d6ba1-cc1d-4745-8aa7-e4b1183a230b][e51d6ba1]]* — the heading
whose job was to decide whether this one is superseded. It is not, and
three separate things now depend on it, which is more than "someday"
can carry:

1. Observed *live on 2026-08-12*, after the cutover, so it is residue of
   nothing.
2. It is the upstream half of the causal chain to =803314aa=, per the
   paragraph directly above.
3. [[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]]'s decision to
   *keep* =claude-code-ide-org-close-open-interval= rests explicitly on
   this heading — a shipped decision now cites it as a reason.

Via =org-clock-idle-time=, already built into org — just needs

No config.el/config-test.el changes — there's no wall-clock idle to

** MAYBE Coarsen :SESSIONS: to session-level, not turn-level :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       c6b598ed-9e92-4a3a-99fb-9c5194d290e4
:CREATED:  [2026-07-28 Tue 11:06]
:END:
:SESSIONS: currently inherits the same Stop/UserPromptSubmit hooks as
:LOGBOOK:, so every turn boundary writes a Paused/Resumed pair — fine
for CLOCK, whose "many short intervals instead of one long one"
granularity is intentional (see the dual-timekeeping design note), but
not what :SESSIONS: was meant to show: the *session-level* bracketing
arc, not per-prompt noise. Wanted instead: a genuine close only at
DOING → DONE with the work tested and committed, at the end of a
working day, or on an explicit signal — sketched as a command like
=/dfd <task>= ("done for the day").

*Open question:* does "end of a working day" need its own detection
(e.g. against =claude-code-ide-org-working-hours=), or is =/dfd=
always a manual call — auto-detecting day boundaries crosses into
guessing user intent, exactly the kind of call CLAUDE.md's existing
rules say only the model/user should make, not a hook.

** MAYBE Consider archives in org_clock_report's scope (agenda-with-archives) :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       0465c1d5-a064-4c8f-8082-9b853377ddf6
:CREATED:  [2026-07-29 Wed 12:25]
:END:
Deferred out of the tracked-files fix above: once =DONE.org= (or any
archive file) is ever reachable from
=claude-code-ide-org--tracked-files=, retrospective =org_clock_report=
calls without an =:ID:= should account for archived work's clocked
time too — completed tasks' =:LOGBOOK:= history moves into =DONE.org=
on archive, so excluding it undercounts. Not urgent now: =DONE.org=
isn't symlinked into =~/org= today, so it isn't in scope either way.

Org's clocktable machinery already has a built-in mechanism for
exactly this — the =:scope= parameter accepts ='agenda-with-archives=
(or ='file-with-archives=), which follows each agenda file's
=#+ARCHIVE:=/=org-archive-location= automatically. Prefer that over
symlinking =DONE.org= alongside =TODO.org= in =~/org= if/when this
becomes worth building — it keeps the interactive prospective agenda
and the retrospective clock-report scope independently correct
instead of coupled to the same file list.

** MAYBE Worktree-based session partitioning, with a canonical-file caveat :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       2e09adb7-6e29-499c-a40e-98cd2b327cff
:CREATED:  [2026-08-04 Tue 13:29]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-04 Tue 13:30] \\
  Auto-promoted: sole remaining TODO in its sibling group.
:END:
[[file:~/.claude/plans/worktree-session-partitioning.md][Plan]]

Raised in conversation 2026-08-04: can separate Claude Code sessions
work on distinct TODOs simultaneously by each operating in its own
=git worktree= of a project repo? For the *code* side, yes — that's
exactly what worktrees are for, one checkout + branch per session,
no shared-file collisions. Emacs' own uniquify already disambiguates
same-basename buffers at different paths, so that part isn't even in
question.

Where it doesn't extend: =TODO.org=/=DONE.org= themselves must stay a
single canonical copy in the main checkout, not one copy per
worktree. Two failure modes rule out a per-worktree copy: (1) =:ID:=
lookups go through =org-id-locations=, a single global hash table
keyed by ID — a heading =:ID:= duplicated across two on-disk copies
(which a worktree checkout produces automatically) means whichever
copy was scanned last silently wins, with every =org_*= tool
targeting an unpredictable copy; (2) CLOCK/=:SESSIONS:= data written
into a worktree-local copy is invisible to =org_clock_report=/
=org_query=/the agenda until that branch merges, and is lost outright
if the worktree is torn down first — fragmenting exactly the
attention-tracking data the clock exists to keep whole. The single
clock living in one Emacs process reading one canonical file is the
mechanism that enforces "one attention at a time," not a limitation
to route around.

Related: [[id:337f7fb2-b9e9-4c02-82dd-d88e60df364b][Concurrent Claude Code sessions share one global org-clock with no session identity]]
already solved the narrower "which session owns the running clock"
problem this would otherwise collide with. This entry is scoped to
whether/how to formalize worktrees-for-code as a documented pattern
(e.g. in CLAUDE.md's Emacs integration section) alongside that
existing single-canonical-file constraint — not yet decided as worth
building anything for; may just be a documentation note rather than
code.

*Update 2026-08-05:* the core premise here — "the single clock is the
mechanism that enforces one attention at a time, not a limitation to
route around" — is exactly what's being stress-tested by real
concurrent-session activity found this same day:
[[id:582cc7f4-41a2-4666-ad3f-1b76b459147e][session-resume blindly inherits any session's last-clocked heading]],
[[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][Direct file writes to a live org buffer can desync org-clock-marker]],
and
[[id:d150c02e-06fe-4514-bf69-e2cc5d8df4c7][org_clock_in doesn't record clock ownership, unlike session-resume]].
Not reopening the conclusion here — just flagging that if/when this
gets planned, it should be read against that fresh evidence, not just
the original 2026-07-30 reasoning.

** TODO Stale entries in org-clock's persisted history hang the first clock-in after a restart :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       001fb310-69f7-4417-81be-a224a3bf83e0
:CREATED:  [2026-08-10 Mon 11:51]
:END:

Hit live 2026-08-10 while setting =f9f61c04= to =DOING=. The
=org_set_todo= MCP call timed out, while Emacs itself stayed responsive
to =emacsclient -e= -- a recursive edit still runs a command loop, so
the server keeps answering. That combination makes a blocked Emacs look
like a tool fault.

*Mechanism, fully root-caused:* Doom advises =org-clock-in= with
=+org--clock-load-a=, a =:before= advice that lazily calls
=org-clock-load= on the *first* clock-in of an Emacs session.
=org-clock-load= visits every file named in the persisted history
(=org-clock-persist-file=, here
=~/.config/emacs/.local/etc/org-clock-save.el=). That history still
named =/private/tmp/planning-verify/scratch.org=, a scratch file from an
earlier verification session whose directory /tmp reaping had since
removed. Visiting it produced a buffer whose save asked
=Directory .../planning-verify/ does not exist! Create it? (y or n)= --
and a =y-or-n-p= is *not* an error, so no =condition-case= in this
module could have caught it. It simply waited.

*Why it first looked like leaked state:* a separate =emacsclient= query
reported =claude-code-ide-org--auto-clock-in-active= as =t=, which reads
exactly like a re-entrancy guard that failed to unwind. It was not --
the =let=-binding was still live on the blocked call's stack. Reading it
that way is what located the hang inside =--trigger-auto-clock-in=, and
it generalizes: a dynamic binding observed as set from outside means the
frame that bound it has not returned.

*Cleared 2026-08-10*: =abort-recursive-edit=, killed the stale buffer,
filtered =org-clock-history= of markers into nonexistent files, and
called =org-clock-save= to rewrite the persist file from the cleaned
live history. Verified -- the file no longer mentions the path, and the
next =org_clock_in= returned immediately.

*What is NOT fixed, and is the actual task here:* nothing prevents a
recurrence. Any verification pass that clocks into a scratch org file
under /tmp writes that path into the persisted history, and the first
clock-in after the next restart hangs once /tmp is reaped. =bin/test=
runs =emacs --batch -Q= and cannot pollute the real history, but hand
verification in a live Emacs does exactly this -- and
[[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]] established that
live verification is *necessary* here, because batch behaves
differently. Options, unevaluated: prune nonexistent files via an
=org-clock-load= advice; keep live-verification scratch files somewhere
/tmp reaping does not reach; or set =org-clock-persist= to nil and
accept losing clock history across restarts.

Relevant to [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]]: a
hang in the clock path is the exact failure class that refactor exists
to eliminate, and this one survives the cutover untouched, since the
apply command clocks in through the same advised =org-clock-in=.

** DONE Refactor clock/TODO-state driving to an append-only event queue + interactive batch-apply review :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644
:CREATED:  [2026-08-06 Thu 10:47]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-06 Thu 12:07] \\
  Auto-promoted: sole remaining TODO in its sibling group.
CLOCK: [2026-08-06 Thu 15:45]--[2026-08-06 Thu 16:50] =>  1:05
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-06 Thu 22:35]
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 11:10]--[2026-08-11 Tue 11:13] =>  0:03
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 13:58]--[2026-08-14 Fri 14:06] =>  0:08
- [2026-08-14 Fri 13:58]--[2026-08-14 Fri 14:06] reallocated from e51d6ba1's mis-suggested span

[[file:~/.claude/plans/plan-this-task-clever-cupcake.md][Plan]]

Captured 2026-08-06 from conversation, after a frank assessment of
this whole clock/session-identity subsystem concluded the *original*
idea — Claude Code sessions driving live org state (TODO keyword,
clock, =:LOGBOOK:=/=:SESSIONS:= logging) directly and synchronously,
in real time, across concurrent/background sessions — is not bearing
fruit. Evidence, not just a vibe: in roughly a day and a half of real
use this produced two distinct clock-marker desync incidents
([[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][Direct file writes to a
live org buffer can desync org-clock-marker]]), a malformed bare
=CLOCK:= line from concurrent writers, an idle-clock-hijack bug
([[id:582cc7f4-41a2-4666-ad3f-1b76b459147e][session-resume blindly
inherits any session's last-clocked heading]]), a clock-ownership
gap covering 2 of 3 clock-open paths
([[id:d150c02e-06fe-4514-bf69-e2cc5d8df4c7][org_clock_in doesn't
record clock ownership, unlike session-resume]]), a zero-time-clock
erasure bug (=293ac49e=, DONE), a genuine-looking-but-actually-noise
bookkeeping delta needing human cleanup after the fact (commits
=4cc769f=/=bc45342=), and — the proximate trigger for this whole
heading — discovering that
[[id:04d0e7d5-ab6b-4972-925d-d517484c7595][native org state-change
logging]] was implemented, then found live to hang Emacs
indefinitely when triggered non-interactively via =emacsclient -e=,
and so was quietly suppressed for every tool-driven transition ever
since. Root cause, not a string of unrelated bugs: org's clock/
logging model assumes one human, one buffer, one synchronous
keypress at a time. This project's actual workload — concurrent
sessions, background agents, non-interactive =emacsclient= calls, a
global clock paused/resumed across turn boundaries — is the opposite
of that, and every incident above traces back to the same mismatch.
See [[id:c954f650-b92f-4920-9967-ab6c44577b30][Revisit the PLANNING
TODO-state's design and whether it's worth keeping]] (=MAYBE=,
captured the same day) — this heading substantially supersedes that
one's question rather than answering it in place, since a working
version of this redesign would make most of =PLANNING='s current
fragility moot.

*Proposed redesign:* Claude Code sessions stop touching the live
Emacs buffer for anything clock- or state-sensitive. Instead they
append plain, dumb, append-only JSON events to a per-session file
(e.g. =~/.claude/org-updates/<session_id>.jsonl=) — no buffer edits,
no clock mutation, no non-interactive logging trigger, most of it not
even requiring Emacs to be reachable to write. A new interactive
Emacs command reads the accumulated events across all pending session
files, presents them to the human as a reviewable batch (think
=org-agenda='s bulk-mark UI or =magit-status='s staging area, not a
raw diff), and applies the approved subset through org's *real*,
synchronous, single-actor machinery — =org-todo=, =org-clock-in=,
=org-clock-out= — called for real, inside a genuinely interactive
command, so native =!=/=@= logging finally just works instead of
needing to be suppressed.

*Not everything moves* — only the two categories that have actually
caused every incident: TODO-state transitions and clock start/stop.

| Stays direct, as today                                    | Moves to queue + review              |
|------------------------------------------------------------+---------------------------------------|
| =org_query= (read-only)                                    | =org_set_todo= (all states — needed for logging to fire, not just clock states) |
| =org_clock_report= (read-only)                              | =org_clock_in= / =org_clock_out=       |
| =org_refile=, =org_archive=, =org_move_sibling=, =org_sort_children= (never implicated in any incident) | Stop/UserPromptSubmit session-pause/resume signals (become bare timestamp appends — no Emacs call, no owner variable, no cross-session race, since there's no shared live clock left to race on) |
| =org_capture= (heading creation — separate, orthogonal bugs already noted elsewhere) | |
| =org_log_background_plan= (already deliberately clock/state-free — already exactly this pattern) | |

*What this eliminates* (mapped against the evidence above): marker
desync (no writer but the human-driven apply command ever touches
=TODO.org=/=DONE.org= — *false as written, see the correction below*),
the idle-clock-hijack and missing-ownership
bugs (moot — ownership is just the queue filename, no shared clock to
inherit), the malformed-concurrent-write =CLOCK:= corruption (exactly
one writer, one operation at a time), the noise-vs-genuine bookkeeping
judgment call (reviewed *before* anything lands, with session/timing
visible, instead of reconstructed from a git diff after the fact —
[[id:782cda6c-111c-454e-93eb-b2b27012078b][Audit for clock/session
bookkeeping noise before committing during a clocking-suppressed
session]]'s mechanism-approach likely folds into the new review UI
rather than standing alone), and the non-interactive logging hang that
started this whole thread.

*Three children were promoted out of this epic on 2026-08-13, and the
test that moved them is worth keeping.* Ask not "is this related?" —
everything here is related — but *"would this epic be incomplete without
it?"*

- [[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]] carries a =+1m=
  repeater, so it never reaches =DONE=. An epic containing it can never
  be =DONE= either — and
  [[id:38b92521-bba4-4bb5-9cd9-465862471a72][38b92521]] is
  =:BLOCKER:=-ed on this epic, so one misplaced child had silently
  frozen an unrelated task. That is the concrete cost of getting this
  wrong, not a matter of taste.
- [[id:b5f94b88-76e3-4a3c-ac73-729f34d33691][b5f94b88]] has its own plan
  file, its own scope decisions and its own deferred phases. A child
  with a plan file of its own is usually an epic wearing a child's
  clothes.
- [[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]] is a capability
  this epic *enables* rather than one it requires.

Everything else added late — eight defects found in this epic's own
shipped code — stayed, and rightly: discovering that your review command
fabricates intervals is not scope creep, it is the work.

They were promoted to level 2 in this category rather than gathered
under a new epic. Grouping them would have grouped by *timing* ("both
are next"), which is the same error as filing them here — proximity
mistaken for membership. An epic should earn its parenthood by having
children that must complete for it to complete; neither of those two has
children yet, and =b5f94b88= will become one naturally if its capture
and amend halves ever split.

*Correction 2026-08-13 to the marker-desync claim above, which is
load-bearing and false.* "No writer but the human-driven apply command
ever touches =TODO.org=/=DONE.org=" was never true and still is not.
=org_capture=, =org_refile=, =org_archive=, =org_move_sibling= and
=org_sort_children= all still write those files directly and
immediately; the org skill text-edits them routinely with Claude Code's
=Edit= tool, which does not go through Emacs at all; git rewrites them
under a live buffer with =global-auto-revert-mode= on; and
=--trigger-auto-clock-in= was deliberately *kept*, so a human's own
=C-c C-t= still opens a clock.

The cutover removed the *clock and state* writers, not the *text*
writers. Marker desync is therefore not eliminated — it was observed
live 2026-08-12, and reading =org-clock.el= on 2026-08-13 showed it is
the reachable trigger for
[[id:803314aa-eda9-4d48-9e43-b3eb4802a4e8][803314aa]]'s fabricated
interval, via =(error "Clock start time is gone")=. Anyone cancelling
[[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][53b0047d]] by citing the
sentence above would be retiring a live bug on the strength of a claim
this heading never established.

*What it costs, honestly:* real-time org state lags until a review pass runs
(mitigate by pointing the statusline at the *queue* files instead of live clock
state — see sub-task below); adds a manual review step to the human's workflow
(keep it cheap — one keybinding); and this **likely obsoletes, in their
currently-planned form**, [[file:~/.claude/plans/clock-marker-desync-guard.md][=53b0047d='s plan]], [[file:~/.claude/plans/session-resume-continuity-guard.md][=582cc7f4='s plan]], and [[file:~/.claude/plans/clock-in-ownership-recording.md][=d150c02e='s
plan]] — each patches a problem this architecture makes structurally impossible
rather than mitigated. Not wasted work: each documents its failure mode
precisely, which is exactly the input this redesign is answering. [[file:~/.claude/plans/sessions-logbook-ordering.md][=2b0988ed='s
plan]] (entry ordering) most likely still applies independent of this refactor,
worth the evaluator's own judgment either way.

*Load-bearing assumption, explicitly flagged for early verification,
not assumed true:* this design only works if the org functions the
apply command calls (=org-clock-in=, =org-clock-out=, whatever drives
=org-add-log-note=/=org-store-log-note=) actually accept — or can be
made to accept — an explicit *retrospective* timestamp, so a batch
applied at 5pm can still backfill a =CLOCK:= interval and a state-
change log note at the times the underlying events actually happened
at 9am, 11am, etc., not "now." If that turns out to be false or only
partially true for some of these functions, significant parts of the
design below need rethinking before implementation, not after.

Nested below as separate sub-tasks, deliberately broken out for
readability rather than left as one undifferentiated blob — each gets
its own =:ID:= and starts as =TODO=; sequencing among them (which
should happen first) is left to whoever evaluates this, not fixed
here, except where a sub-task's own text says otherwise.

Triaged 2026-08-06, later the same day: they no longer all sit at
=TODO=. =c9292ce8= (read-time overlay) is =CANCELLED= as working
against this refactor's own premise; =16160d23= (per-subagent
intervals) is =MAYBE=, blocked on =3d576d29=; =7771fc63= was added for
the stale-interval-recovery feature, whose premise this design removes.
=3d576d29='s clock half is now confirmed answered and its scope
narrowed to the logging half. Each carries its own note; this is just
the index.

*Plan staleness audited 2026-08-11.* This epic's own plan file has been
superseded on most of its concrete decisions by the two later plans, and
nothing in it says so. A reader reaching for it first — which is what an
epic-level plan invites — would be misled on the queue path, the schema,
attribution and apply bookkeeping alike:

| the plan says                                                                   | what was built                                                                |
|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| queue at =<repo>/.claude/org-updates/=                                          | =~/.claude/org-updates/=, global                                              |
| =session_id= as explicit tool arguments                                         | the hook writes it; the tool never sees it                                    |
| missing =session_id= lands in =unattributed.jsonl=, "never dropped"             | the event is dropped outright                                                 |
| every event carries a UUID =event_id=; =applied= events append to the same file | no =event_id= exists; applied state is a sibling =.applied= set               |
| schema =(event_id, heading_id, payload)=                                        | =(ts, kind, id, state, from, note, session_id, agent_id, agent_type, source)= |
| an elisp writer plus =bin/org-queue-append=                                     | shell only, at =bin/hooks/queue-append=                                       |
| retire =--trigger-auto-clock-in=                                                | kept, and suppressed during apply via the re-entrancy guard                   |
| branch =feature/event-queue-review=                                             | =feature/event-queue-format=                                                  |

*And one live contradiction, not merely staleness.* The plan's decision 5
records the =:SESSIONS:= drawer's retirement as *confirmed with the
user*. [[id:9d2fcdad-9bf7-47b6-8018-223b13ec4577][9d2fcdad]], written a
day later, treats the same question as deliberately deferred and
=MAYBE=. Both cannot be current, and the disagreement is load-bearing:
it decides whether a whole subsystem gets deleted.

Until the plan is revised, =event-queue-format.md= and
=review-and-cutover.md= are authoritative where they disagree with it —
they are later and were written against real code.

*Closed 2026-08-15, nine days after capture.* Final tally: 29 =DONE=
children, 2 =CANCELLED=, and 9 headings promoted out to =**= over the
epic's life rather than absorbed.

*What shipped, against what was proposed above.* The redesign landed as
described: sessions no longer touch the live buffer for clock- or
state-sensitive work. =org_set_todo= changes no keyword, =org_clock_in=
opens no clock, =org_clock_out= closes none — each appends to
=~/.claude/org-updates/<session_id>.jsonl= through
=bin/hooks/queue-append=, the sole writer. =M-x
claude-code-ide-org-review= reads across pending session files and
applies the approved subset through =org-todo=/=org-clock-in=/
=org-clock-out= inside a genuinely interactive command.

*The proximate trigger is resolved.* Native =!=/=@= state-change logging
was suppressed for every tool-driven transition because it hung Emacs
when driven through =emacsclient -e=. It now runs at apply time, inside a
real interactive command, and nothing is suppressed. The hang was never
fixed — it was routed around, which was the point.

*Grew well past the proposal in three directions the plan did not
anticipate:* guidepost reconstruction of spans from =pause=/=resume=
with a tuned gap threshold; span evidence at review time (commits and
=:CREATED:= stamps inside a span's window, which turned out to beat both
transcript-reading and model assistance); and permission-block
bracketing, since a turn stalled on a prompt never ends and so was
credited as work.

*Verified continuously rather than at the end:* 277 ERT tests plus four
shell suites, and real use — 23 unattributed spans assigned by hand in
one sitting, and repeated apply passes since.

*What it deliberately did not settle, and this is the honest part.* The
queue records *agent* activity and has never recorded the user's own
attention; that distinction only got its vocabulary on 2026-08-15 —
*clock intervals* measured, *attention intervals* attested — and the
second half is [[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]],
untouched. Defects found in the machinery after it shipped remain open:
[[id:0d055205-a670-45c6-b4af-980961603a7c][0d055205]],
[[id:49cbe319-f7db-413a-ade8-c4850c1fab8c][49cbe319]],
[[id:09c134c4-31eb-4f6c-aa10-f836d1d28f16][09c134c4]] and
[[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]].

*Closed over two =MAYBE= children rather than by finishing them* —
[[id:118373e5-b89b-4001-ad52-1f68374745fb][118373e5]] and
[[id:0c8644ff-f7b1-4789-ac1e-babf2e9fbc8e][0c8644ff]] were promoted out
on 2026-08-15 on the reasoning this heading applied seven times before:
a capability the epic *enables* is not one it *requires*, and an epic
that keeps every downstream capability can never close. Both remain
=MAYBE= with their triggers written down.

Unblocks [[id:38b92521-bba4-4bb5-9cd9-465862471a72][38b92521]].


*** DONE Design the append-only event-queue format and per-session file lifecycle :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       32272061-1d78-4726-b13b-90338edb2ba5
:CREATED:  [2026-08-06 Thu 10:47]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-06 Thu 22:55]--[2026-08-07 Fri 08:20] =>  9:25
- State "DOING"      from "PLANNING"   [2026-08-07 Fri 07:59] \\
  Auto-promoted: ExitPlanMode fired on the owning session.
:END:

[[file:~/.claude/plans/event-queue-format.md][Plan]]

JSONL schema per event kind (=todo=, =clock_in=, =clock_out=,
=pause=, =resume=, at minimum), file naming/location
(=~/.claude/org-updates/<session_id>.jsonl= proposed, not settled),
and lifecycle after a successful apply (delete vs. truncate vs.
append an =applied= marker so a session can keep writing after a
partial drain without needing to know if it was fully consumed).
Crash-safety: a session dying mid-stream should leave its queue file
harmless and inert, never corrupt, never lost.

Note added 2026-08-06: =pause=/=resume= events are not just historical
bookkeeping — per
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s design, they
become the *guideposts* a human uses to reconstruct their own real
=CLOCK:= intervals at review time. Their timestamp density directly
determines how useful that reconstruction is, so keep emitting one
per real turn boundary (matching today's =Stop=/=UserPromptSubmit=
granularity) rather than coarsening them for this design's sake —
coarser guideposts make the human's own reconstruction job harder,
not easier.

*Open question to settle here, raised 2026-08-06: do =clock_in=/
=clock_out= events survive at all for human-attended sessions?* Follow
the design through and they are demoted from timing records to bare
*intent markers*. For a human session the interval can't be applied
from them anyway — [[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]
requires the human to draw it from =pause=/=resume= guideposts, which
are strictly denser and better-founded than a =clock_in= event. The one
place a =clock_in=/=clock_out= *pair* still carries authoritative
timing is the subagent path, and that path is now deferred
([[id:16160d23-273b-4510-858e-db18508d04a0][16160d23]], =MAYBE=), so
nothing in v1 consumes them as timing data at all. Two live options:
1. *Keep them as intent markers.* Their remaining job is heading
   attribution — telling the review UI which heading Claude believed it
   was on, so guideposts can be grouped when several tasks interleave
   inside one session. The guidepost stream alone does not solve that,
   which is the real argument for keeping them.
2. *Drop them for v1.* =org_set_todo= → =DOING= already carries the same
   intent, and pause/resume already carry the timing. Smaller event
   schema, and it lets CLAUDE.md's state-transition table lose the
   "always call =org_clock_in=" rule entirely, since there'd be no
   clock to open.

Leaning (1) on the interleaving argument, but genuinely undecided and
worth settling before the schema is written rather than after.

*Related, decide alongside it:* even as queue-appends, the MCP tools
still execute inside Emacs — they're served by the Emacs tools server
(=.mcp.json=, =claude-code-ide-emacs-tools-setup=), so =org_clock_in=
reaches Emacs and appends a line there. Harmless (no buffer mutation,
no clock state), but it is *not* the "no Emacs required to write"
property the parent heading claims — only the hooks get that, per
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]]'s "no
=emacsclient= call at all." Whether the clock/state tools should also
write the queue file directly, bypassing the tools server so they work
with Emacs down, is unrecorded elsewhere and belongs to this sub-task's
file-lifecycle design.

*Both resolved during planning, 2026-08-07.* =clock_in=/=clock_out=
events are kept, on the basis the user set out: they exist to inform the
human's composition of =CLOCK:= entries at review time, contributing
only *active* timestamps to =:LOGBOOK:=, subject to aggregation at
ingestion so review stays manageable. The decisive technical argument is
attribution — =pause=/=resume= are heading-less, and a return to a
heading that never left =DOING= emits no =todo= event, so without them
every subsequent guidepost silently attributes to the wrong heading.
On the write path: the *hook* writes and the *tool* validates, since
=session_id= only ever appears on hook stdin and never reaches an MCP
tool.

*Implementation notes (2026-08-07):* shipped the schema, the writer and
the reader. =bin/hooks/queue-append= is the single shared writer, called
with the event kind as =$1= and the hook payload on stdin; it builds the
line entirely with =jq -n= (same reasoning as =session-pause='s existing
comment), refuses a =session_id= that is not a plain filename component,
and drops any =todo=/=clock_in=/=clock_out= whose =tool_response= begins
with ="Error:"= — =--at-id= returns error strings rather than throwing,
so that is the only signal a bad =:ID:= never resolved. On the Emacs
side, a new "Event queue" section adds
=claude-code-ide-org-queue-directory= and
=claude-code-ide-org-guidepost-gap-threshold= (default 15 minutes,
explicitly a starting guess) plus =--queue-events=,
=--queue-events-by-id=, =--aggregate-guideposts= and the watermark
functions. Draining never touches the =.jsonl=: a sibling
=<session-id>.applied= watermark records progress, so a session that is
still appending is never raced.

*Verified, not just implemented:* =bin/test= passes 134/134 (124
pre-existing + 10 new ERT tests) covering round-trip of all five kinds,
skipping torn/malformed/unknown-kind lines without failing the file,
watermark filtering including a session that keeps appending after a
partial drain, atomicity of the watermark write, the =A → B → A=
attribution case, cross-session isolation, gap clustering at two
thresholds, and that a 2-minute span survives where
=--consolidate-logbook-text= drops it (asserted as a test, not a
comment). =bin/queue-append-test= adds 20 shell assertions on the writer
— field mapping per kind, ="Error:"= suppression, missing/traversing
=session_id=, unparseable payload, append accumulation, per-session
files, always-exit-0. Also ran a cross-boundary pass neither suite
covers: the real hook script wrote a nine-event =A → B → A= stream which
the real Emacs reader then attributed correctly, drained 9 → 0 via the
watermark, and left the queue file intact at nine lines.

*Deviations from the plan, all deliberate:*
- =session-pause=/=session-resume= *keep* their =emacsclient= call and
  append to the queue alongside it, rather than dropping it as the plan
  said. Dropping it now would stop pausing the live clock at turn
  boundaries while the MCP tools still open real clocks — every interval
  would silently absorb idle time, with nothing yet able to apply the
  queued events instead. Additive now, removed in =feba67eb='s cutover.
- =--aggregate-guideposts= does *not* reuse
  =--merge-time-intervals= as the plan proposed: that merges only
  touching or overlapping intervals and has no gap tolerance, which is a
  different question from clustering nearby points.
- =--parse-iso8601= shape-checks its input before calling
  =date-to-time=. A test caught that =date-to-time= accepts ="nonsense"=
  and returns a near-epoch time, which would sort a garbage line *ahead*
  of every real event — corrupting ordering rather than being skipped.
- Added a generic =--atomic-write= rather than reusing
  =--write-clock-status=, which is hardwired to its own defcustom path.
  Folding the older function onto the new helper is a tidy-up left for
  whoever next touches the statusline.

*Amended 2026-08-07, after the schema was checked against
=clock-template.org=.* The original design had no =note= field, so a
drained queue would have produced bare =CLOCK:= lines and unlabelled
=State "X" from "Y"= lines — a strictly poorer =:LOGBOOK:= than the
template's conventions call for, where every non-=CLOCK:= line carries a
short label. A real gap, missed because the schema was designed against
this heading's own text rather than against the template that defines
the target output.

Added: a nullable =note= field, populated from =tool_input.note=, with
an optional =note= argument on =org_clock_in=, =org_clock_out= and
=org_set_todo= (3-10 words). The Elisp wrappers accept and *ignore* it
deliberately — writing it into the live buffer now would produce exactly
the unreviewed record this design exists to avoid, and there is no
correct place for it in a =:LOGBOOK:= until a human confirms the
interval it belongs to. At apply time it becomes the =\\= continuation
on org's native state-change line, via the =org-store-log-note= path
already confirmed in [[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]]
(the note text is the note buffer's contents; empty yields a bare State
line).

Also fixed =clock_out='s null =id=. The tool takes no =id= argument —
the running clock is the only thing that knows which heading is closing
— so its reply now reports the id it *actually* closed
(="Clocked out: \"H\" (id: ID)"=) and =queue-append= recovers it from
there. Authoritative in a way a caller-supplied argument would not be:
it cannot disagree with what happened. The ="Clocked out:"= prefix is
unchanged, which is all =bin/clock-notify= matches on.

Still open, deliberately: *guidepost span labels*. In the template a
label like =- <09:00>--<09:15> clarify backend schema design= belongs to
a *span*, aggregated from many =pause=/=resume= events — and those come
from hooks Claude never invokes, so no call site can supply one. Default
plan is to inherit the enclosing =clock_in='s note, leaving spans
unlabelled where there is none, with transcript-derived labels
([[id:0c8644ff-f7b1-4789-ac1e-babf2e9fbc8e][0c8644ff]]) as the eventual
upgrade. Worth noting that deferral is a scope call, *not* a capability
gap: reading a transcript is plain file I/O, locatable from =session_id=
alone, and needs none of the MCP sampling machinery
=claude-code-ide= lacks.

Verified: =bin/test= 138/138 (4 new), =bin/queue-append-test= 30/30 (10
new), and =config.el= live-reloaded with the new arities and tool schema
confirmed in the running Emacs.


*Plan staleness audited 2026-08-11.* =event-queue-format.md= has drifted
from what shipped in four places:
- *The watermark design was abandoned.* The plan describes
  =<session_id>.applied= as holding "the =ts= of the last applied
  event," with everything at or before it treated as consumed. The
  implementation stores an applied *set*, because a high-water mark
  cannot advance past a skipped item — observed live 2026-08-07, when a
  real apply wrote no watermark at all and would have re-proposed
  everything.
- *It states the ="Error:"= suppression as working.* It does not, for
  the response shape a real failing MCP call produces; see
  [[id:5e2a1c04-7b3f-4d21-9c88-2f6e0a91b7d3][5e2a1c04]]. The plan
  documents an intent the code does not achieve, which is worse than
  documenting nothing.
- *The schema table is missing three fields* added since: =note= (the
  amendment covers it), =agent_type=, and =from=.
- *The "no auto-rounding" rationale points at machinery that no longer
  exists.* Rounding was retired outright in
  [[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]]; the conclusion
  still holds but the reason cited for it, and the =config.el:900/991=
  line references, do not.
