[[file:~/.claude/plans/review-and-cutover.md][Plan]]

Planned 2026-08-07 jointly with
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]] as one two-phase
plan (this heading is Phase B); see that heading for why they share one
plan file. The =:BLOCKER:= is not bookkeeping: the moment these tools
stop mutating live state, something has to apply the queued events, and
that something is =720b2dcf=. Landing this first would leave every
transition and interval queued with no way to reach an org file.

*Settled during planning:* the tool-side guards are *retired* rather
than made queue-aware — =bin/hooks/pretooluse-transition-guard= and
=bin/clock-notify= (with =bin/clock-notify-test=) both exist to police
live state against concurrent writers, and after the cutover there are
none. Human review becomes the validation step, which is the point of
the refactor. The org-side hooks (=org-blocker-hook=/=org-trigger-hook=)
stay: they still guard genuine hand-edits made directly in Emacs.

=org_set_todo=, =org_clock_in=, =org_clock_out= become queue-appends
instead of live buffer mutations (per the routing table above).
=bin/hooks/session-pause=/=session-resume= become bare timestamp
appends with no =emacsclient= call at all — this alone is what
retires the =337f7fb2=/=582cc7f4=/=d150c02e= ownership-variable
infrastructure, so double-check nothing else still depends on
=claude-code-ide-org--clock-owner-session-id=/
=--planning-owner-session-id= before deleting them.

Three existing =PostToolUse=/=PreToolUse= hooks don't just port over
unchanged and are easy to overlook since they aren't in the routing
table above (that table is about MCP tools, not the hook layer
sitting alongside them):
- =bin/hooks/pretooluse-transition-guard= validates a transition
  against the heading's *current* state before allowing it — but
  "current" on disk goes stale the moment anything for that heading
  is queued-but-unapplied. Needs to become queue-aware (validate
  against the latest *queued* state for that heading, not disk
  state) or it will wrongly allow/reject transitions once a queue
  exists.
- =bin/clock-notify= exists to cross-check a tool's claimed clock
  result against live =(org-clocking-p)=. Once =org_clock_in=/
  =org_clock_out= never touch live clock state at all, that
  comparison is meaningless — retire it or repurpose it into
  something like "you have N unreviewed updates" instead of "clock
  state mismatch."
- Three clock settings in the user's *personal Doom config*
  (=~/.config/doom/config.el=), added 2026-08-06 — outside this repo,
  which is exactly why they're easy to miss when sweeping the repo for
  clock callers. All three become dead code, because after this
  refactor a clock is only ever live for the microseconds between the
  paired =org-clock-in=/=org-clock-out= calls inside the apply command:
  =claude-code-ide-org--clock-out-if-clocking= on =kill-emacs-hook= and
  on =suspend-hook= (config.el:88-91) exist to catch a clock left
  running across a quit or suspend — =(org-clocking-p)= will
  essentially never be true at those moments any more; and
  =org-clock-out-when-done t= (config.el:67), which fires on =org-todo=
  → =DONE=, now only ever runs inside apply where the clock is already
  closed. Removing them is optional (they're harmless no-ops), but
  leaving them in place misleads the next reader into thinking a
  background clock still exists.
- =bin/hooks/exitplanmode-promote-planning= auto-promotes =PLANNING=
  → =DOING= by reusing the same live clock interval. Under this
  design that becomes just another queued =todo= event stacked on
  the still-unapplied =PLANNING= one — fine, but it means the
  apply-time reconciliation (see the review-and-apply command below)
  has to correctly collapse a =TODO→PLANNING→DOING= event sequence
  into one coherent interval, not replay each event as if
  independent. Concrete supporting case for why the backdated-
  timestamp sub-task below matters as much as it does.


*Plan staleness audited 2026-08-11.* Phase B of =review-and-cutover.md=
inherits the stale watermark and rounding passages noted on
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]], and has a
structural weakness of its own: it is a *list of edits*, not a sequenced
plan. Phase A gets ordering, hook interactions and failure modes; Phase B
gets bullet points. For the epic's single behavior-changing commit — the
one whose rollback story is "git revert" — that asymmetry is worth
correcting before execution rather than during it.

Specifically unaddressed by the bullets: what order the edits land in so
the suite stays green between them, and what the first session after the
cutover is expected to look like, which is the only real check that the
write paths are all actually severed.

*Working order, derived 2026-08-11 before the first edit.* Not a
re-plan: =review-and-cutover.md='s Phase B decisions are settled and
still hold. What it lacked was an order, which is what follows. One of
its bullets is sequenced wrong, and that is the interesting part.

*The guards must be retired WITH the wrappers, not after.* The plan lists
them as separate bullets, which reads as "whenever." Both become
actively harmful the moment the tools stop mutating:
- =pretooluse-transition-guard= validates a requested transition against
  the heading's *current disk state*. Once a transition only queues, disk
  stops moving — so after queueing =TODO -> DOING=, the guard still sees
  =TODO= and would *reject* the following =DOING -> DONE= as illegal. It
  does not merely go stale; it starts blocking correct work.
- =clock-notify= cross-checks a tool's claimed clock result against live
  =(org-clocking-p)=. Once =org_clock_in= never opens a clock, that
  comparison reports a mismatch on *every* call.

*And session-pause/resume must come after the wrappers, not before.* If
they drop their =emacsclient= call while the tools still open real
clocks, nothing pauses at a turn boundary and every interval inflates
with idle time — the exact reason the additive step was taken during
[[id:32272061-1d78-4726-b13b-90338edb2ba5][32272061]] instead of the
plan's original one-shot.

So:

1. *Wrappers + guards, together.* =org_set_todo=/=org_clock_in=/
   =org_clock_out= validate the =:ID:= and return "queued, pending
   review" without touching a buffer; delete
   =bin/hooks/pretooluse-transition-guard=, =bin/clock-notify= and
   =bin/clock-notify-test= with their =.claude/settings.json= entries.
   Leaves clocking inert, which is correct — apply is the writer now.
2. *=session-pause=/=session-resume=* drop =emacsclient= and become bare
   =queue-append= wrappers.
3. *=exitplanmode-promote-planning=* queues a =todo= event instead of
   driving elisp. Sequenced here because it is the case that most needs
   apply to collapse a =TODO -> PLANNING -> DOING= run into one interval
   rather than replaying three.
4. *Ownership variables* and their machinery
   (=--clock-owner-session-id=, =--planning-owner-session-id=,
   =--maybe-record-planning-owner=, =--promote-planning-to-doing=). Last
   because steps 2 and 3 are what remove their readers; deleting earlier
   would break code still calling them.

Tests move with each step rather than after: their assertions invert from
"the buffer and clock changed" to "an event was appended and the buffer
is untouched," and a suite left asserting the old contract in between
would be noise rather than a signal.

Still one commit, per the plan — the order above is how the edits are
made and the suite is run, not a sequence of commits. =git revert= as
rollback only works if the cutover is atomic.

*Executed 2026-08-11, in the recorded order, one working tree.*

1. *Wrappers + guards.* =org_clock_in= validates the =:ID:= and returns
   ="Queued clock_in on \"H\"; pending review."=; =org_clock_out= returns
   a constant and takes no =:ID:= at all; =org_set_todo= validates the
   =:ID:= *and* the keyword against =org-todo-keywords-1= (buffer-local,
   so it checks what the file declares rather than a list hard-coded in
   the wrapper) and returns ="Queued todo -> DOING (was NEXT): ...="=.
   Deleted =bin/hooks/pretooluse-transition-guard=, =bin/clock-notify=,
   =bin/clock-notify-test= and their =settings.json= entries.
2. *=session-pause=/=session-resume=* are now one =exec= into
   =queue-append= each, with no =emacsclient= call.
3. *=exitplanmode-promote-planning=* reads the session's own queue file
   for the heading it most recently queued =PLANNING= on and appends a
   =DOING= event beside it.
4. *Ownership variables* (=--log-session-id=, =--clock-owner-session-id=,
   =--planning-owner-session-id=) and their machinery
   (=--maybe-record-planning-owner=, =--promote-planning-to-doing=,
   =bin/hooks/posttooluse-record-planning-owner=) deleted.

*Three things went beyond the recorded order, each because leaving them
would have left something that lies:*
- *The one-minute clock floor* (=--minimum-clock-seconds=,
  =--clock-out-floor-time=) went with =clock_out='s body. It was a
  write-path policy and apply is the write path now — and apply
  deliberately writes exact confirmed endpoints, so a floor would be the
  rounding this epic already retired once.
- *The three MCP tool descriptions* — model-facing text still promising
  "Opens a CLOCK entry and starts the Emacs clock timer." Every one now
  says the event is queued and the file does not change, because the
  description is the only thing telling a session not to expect a later
  read to reflect its own call.
- *=queue-append= grew explicit =QUEUE_APPEND_ID/STATE/FROM= overrides*,
  for events with no tool call behind them. The alternative — having
  =exitplanmode= synthesise a fake =tool_response= for the existing sed
  to recover — was rejected as unreadable.

*=clock_out= no longer reports an =:ID:=, and does not need to.* It used
to recover the running clock's heading so =queue-append= could sed it
out of the reply. =--queue-events-by-id= already attributes a null-id
=clock_out= to whichever heading that session's last =clock_in= named,
which is strictly better: the only clock that can be running now belongs
to the *human*, so reading one would attribute a session's interval to
whatever the user happens to be clocking.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 145/145, =bin/queue-append-test= green (12 new
checks, including that an override beats =tool_input= and that both
post-cutover reply shapes still parse), =bin/exitplanmode-promote-test=
green — a *new* harness, 15 checks. That last one is worth naming: the
ExitPlanMode hook had no automated coverage before and could not
reasonably have had any, being a here-doc of elisp handed to
=emacsclient=; it is now a bash read of one queue file, and the
cross-session guard that used to need an in-memory ownership variable is
tested as what it now is — structural, since the file is per-session.

*The test suite inverted rather than shrank.* 28 tests failed on the
first run after the wrappers changed, every one of them a test that used
a wrapper as *setup* ("clock in", "make it DOING") rather than testing
its contract. Two helpers —
=claude-code-ide-org-test--clock-in-for-real= and =--set-todo-for-real=
— now say so explicitly, which makes the remaining wrapper tests
unambiguous: they assert nothing happened. Net 168 → 145 ERT tests: 16
deleted with the code they covered (session pause/resume ownership,
PLANNING promotion, the clock floor, the clock_out id), the rest
rewritten in place.

*Live-verified against the real =TODO.org=, not just batch.* Reloaded
=config.el= and unbound the ten deleted symbols by hand (=load-file=
evaluates what is in a file; it does not unbind what was removed from
it). Then called all three wrappers on this very heading: each returned
its "pending review" string, =set_todo "WAIT"= reported =(was DOING)=
while the heading *stayed* =DOING=, an undeclared keyword and a bad
=:ID:= both came back =Error:=-prefixed — the prefix =queue-append= drops
on — and the file's sha256 was byte-identical before and after with the
buffer unmodified.

*One piece of pre-cutover state was cleaned up deliberately:* the clock
this session opened on this heading at 13:29, before the wrappers
stopped clocking. Nothing would ever have closed it — the =Stop= hook is
queue-only as of this commit — so it was closed through =org-clock-out=,
which is exactly what the old hook would have done at that moment.
Left open it would have tripped the stale-interval recovery check
tomorrow and blocked this heading from reaching =DONE=.

*Not claimed, and the reason this heading is not DONE:* the first
post-cutover session. Everything above is batch, shell, and
=emacsclient=; the plan's own gate for this work is "what the first
session after the cutover looks like," and that cannot happen inside the
session that made the change — =.claude/settings.json= is read once at
session start, so *this* session is still running the old hook wiring,
pointing at three scripts that no longer exist. The first real check is
a fresh session: its =SessionStart=/=UserPromptSubmit= hooks fire the new
scripts, its tool calls queue instead of mutating, and a review pass
applies them.

*Gate met 2026-08-11. Closing.* The next session began 95 seconds after
the cutover commit (=220ba78= 13:50:24, =SessionStart= 13:51:59) and
exercised all three clauses:

- *Hooks fired the new scripts.* Every =UserPromptSubmit= and =Stop= in
  that session produced exactly one =resume=/=pause= in the queue, with
  no spurious or missing entries, matched one-for-one against the
  transcript's own prompt and =stop_hook_summary= records.
- *Tool calls queued instead of mutating.* =org_clock_in=,
  =org_clock_out= and =org_set_todo= were each called against live
  headings; TODO.org's sha256 was unchanged across all of them.
- *A review pass applied them.* Two state transitions were confirmed and
  written with native =State= lines, the watermark advanced by exactly
  those two timestamps, and the =.jsonl= was left intact.

*What the gate caught, which is the point of having had one.* The
session found a live escape of
[[id:5e2a1c04-7b3f-4d21-9c88-2f6e0a91b7d3][5e2a1c04]] — a =clock_in= for
a nonexistent =:ID:= entering the queue, because the =Error:= guard
could not see a content-block =tool_response=. Fixed and closed. Its
downstream damage was then observed for real at the review pass: the
phantom captured a guidepost interval, the heading actually being worked
on recorded nothing, and the 205 seconds proposed were idle time. None
of that was reachable from batch.

*No =clock_out= was queued when closing this heading, deliberately.* The
transition rule that =DOING -> DONE= closes the clock was written for the
live-clocking model. There is no live clock to close; the =clock_in=
queued against this heading at 15:10:36 is orphaned — it produced no
review item at all — and a =clock_out= appended now would be captured by
the same =dead-beef= cursor, manufacturing a fourth permanently
un-appliable queue item. Recorded rather than worked around, since
"what closing a heading means when the clock is a queued concept" is a
real gap in the transition rules that
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]] should settle.

CLOCK: [2026-08-10 Mon 12:55]--[2026-08-10 Mon 13:00] =>  0:05

[[file:~/.claude/plans/review-and-cutover.md][Plan]]

Planned 2026-08-07 jointly with
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]] as one two-phase
plan (this heading is Phase A), because the cutover cannot land until
this command works and most of its requirements fall out of this
design. Both headings link the same plan file — a deliberate,
one-off departure from one-plan-per-heading, taken because two
documents that must agree with each other is the worse failure mode.

The core deliverable: scan pending queue files, reconstruct proposed
per-heading transitions and clock intervals, render a review buffer
(mark/unmark, jump-to-heading, edit-an-interval, apply-marked), and
on apply call the real =org-todo=/=org-clock-in=/=org-clock-out= one
item at a time, synchronously, inside this one command — genuinely
interactive, so native logging fires without the =org-inhibit-
logging= workaround.

**Core design principle, added 2026-08-06 from conversation: two
genuinely different modes, not one uniform "propose an interval"
behavior** — the system cannot tell when a human was actually
reading/thinking/composing versus idle, attending to something else
entirely (another task, email, lunch), between logged interaction
points, so it must not pretend otherwise:
- **Unattended/subagent-derived events**
  ([[id:16160d23-273b-4510-858e-db18508d04a0][16160d23]]) — the
  interval genuinely *is* knowable (a subagent was either running or
  not; =duration_ms= is authoritative), so the apply command can
  propose, and the human can accept in bulk, a real =CLOCK:= entry
  directly. Still reviewed, per the core queue+review model — but
  mechanically derived, not reconstructed.
- **Human-session-derived events** — the apply command must *not*
  auto-write a =CLOCK:= interval on the human's behalf; doing so
  would be exactly the kind of "confidently wrong" record this whole
  redesign exists to avoid. Instead: render each logged =pause=/
  =resume= interaction timestamp
  ([[id:32272061-1d78-4726-b13b-90338edb2ba5][32272061]]) as an
  *active*-timestamp annotation line alongside the heading's =:LOGBOOK:=
  — a "guidepost," not a claim of attention — and leave the actual
  =CLOCK:= interval for the human to draw/type/adjust themselves. A
  suggested default (e.g. span from first to last guidepost in a
  tightly-clustered run) is fine to offer, but must be clearly
  editable/rejectable, never silently committed as fact.

This is also the concrete mechanism for the active-vs-inactive
timestamp convention captured the same day: an *active* annotation
(=<start>--<end> label=) means "a human was logged interacting with
this span, worth a glance in the agenda"; the =CLOCK:= line itself
stays *inactive* as org already does it, unchanged, still the sole
arithmetic source of truth. This lets org's own agenda double as a
free, retrospective "what did I actually attend to" view, no bespoke
code needed for that part — see
[[id:c084553c-0621-4a96-9fa1-f32850aeec6a][c084553c]] for the
verification step this leans on before being trusted.

*Implementation notes (2026-08-07):* =claude-code-ide-org-review= opens
a =*org-review*= buffer in a =special-mode=-derived major mode, grouped
by heading, with =m=/=u=/=t= marking, =e= to edit an interval, =RET= to
jump, =x= to apply marked, =g= to refresh. Items are built by
=--review-items-from-queue=, which classifies the two modes: a
subagent's own paired =clock_in=/=clock_out= becomes an authoritative
interval, while human =pause=/=resume= guideposts cluster into a
=:suggested= span labelled from the enclosing =clock_in='s note.
Applying is =--review-apply-item=, one item at a time.

Three things the apply path gets right on purpose, each traceable to a
specific earlier finding:
- It binds =claude-code-ide-org--auto-clock-in-active=, this module's
  own existing re-entrancy guard, to suppress
  =--trigger-auto-clock-in=. Without it the trigger opens a clock at
  *now* on any → DOING/PLANNING transition, corrupting a backdated
  apply.
- It calls *raw* =org-clock-out=, never
  =claude-code-ide-org-clock-out=, so =consolidate-history='s 5-minute
  rounding never touches a confirmed interval.
- It binds =--log-source= to ="org_review_apply"=, so audit records are
  attributed rather than defaulting to ="hand-edit"= — which matters
  more once apply is the only writer of live state (see
  [[id:9d2fcdad-9bf7-47b6-8018-223b13ec4577][9d2fcdad]]).

*Two findings from verification, both of which changed the code:*
- The note-clobber symptom observed live during
  [[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]] does *not*
  reproduce under =emacs --batch= — the note lands fine there. What
  does reproduce, and is environment-independent, is the stray
  =CLOCK:= at *now*. The regression test asserts that instead, and says
  in its own docstring why it deliberately covers less than the live
  symptom.
- Run against the real 30-event queue, a lone unbracketed guidepost
  produced a zero-width span, which would have written
  =CLOCK: ... =>  0:00=. Now a zero-width span is annotated but writes
  no CLOCK line: one interaction point is not a duration. Distinct from
  the rounding this design rejects — nothing is discarded, because
  there was no interval to discard.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 149/149 (11 new) covering exact backdated
endpoints, a surviving 2-minute interval, active-vs-inactive annotation
per mode, the backdated state line with its note continuation, trigger
suppression both ways, item classification, contiguous-only watermark
advance, clock-then-DONE not tripping the blocker hook, and an
unresolvable =:ID:= being reported rather than thrown. Live-reloaded and
rendered against the real queue: items classify correctly and heading
titles resolve.

*Still owed:* an interactive by-hand pass — marking and applying from
the buffer against a real heading. =3d576d29= established that the
logging path behaves differently under =emacsclient -e= than in batch,
so nothing above proves the interactive apply works end to end. That is
the gate before [[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]].


*Plan staleness audited 2026-08-11.* Phase A of =review-and-cutover.md=
carries the same two drifts as the schema plan — it describes the
watermark as advancing "only to the last *contiguous* applied event"
when the implementation stores an applied set, and it justifies "no
rounding" by pointing at =consolidate-history= machinery that
[[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]] has since removed.

*More consequentially, one item in the plan was silently dropped in
implementation.* The plan's own rendering mockup includes a line reading
=unattributed: 2 guideposts=. Nothing in the command displays those; the
nil-keyed group is skipped whole. Surfacing them is now tracked as
[[id:3d0487f4-b8f8-451c-88bf-ab537663cbd0][3d0487f4]], which should be
read as a *regression against this plan* rather than as a new idea —
it was designed in from the start.

*Gate met, 2026-08-11.* The "still owed" above was an interactive
by-hand pass, on the grounds that
[[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]] showed the logging
path behaves differently under =emacsclient -e= than in batch. It has now
happened three times, and the command survived all three:

- *2026-08-07*, the first pass, which exposed five bugs. Four were fixed
  that day; the fifth was
  [[id:3d93021d-1d8a-4657-bc5c-77734f91fb9d][3d93021d]], root-caused to
  stale global =org-log-note-marker= state and fixed with a one-line
  clear whose reasoning is now a comment.
- *2026-08-10*, a clock item applied cleanly: the reviewed span landed in
  =:LOGBOOK:= with active timestamps and an exact =0:01= CLOCK line. That
  interval was destroyed minutes later, which found
  [[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]] — a bug in
  consolidation, not in apply.
- *2026-08-11*, a real batch of state items, which exercised the
  stale-transition guard in the field. It fired on all four, as it should
  have; they were confirmed at the prompt, which is the override behaving
  as designed.

*What this heading does not close, deliberately.* Two known gaps have
their own headings rather than holding this one open:
- [[id:3d0487f4-b8f8-451c-88bf-ab537663cbd0][3d0487f4]] — unattributed
  guideposts are dropped rather than surfaced. Read that as a *regression
  against this heading's own plan*, whose rendering mockup includes an
  =unattributed: 2 guideposts= line, rather than as a new idea.
- [[id:d409390b-6624-4820-82bb-730d763962fd][d409390b]] — three
  interaction fixes requested after using it for real: mark advances,
  the note is editable, and a dired-style mark-all.

Neither blocks [[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]]:
the cutover needs a command that applies queued events correctly, which
this is, not a comfortable one.
*** DONE Root-cause how apply wrote a DONE nobody queued, onto the wrong heading :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       3d93021d-1d8a-4657-bc5c-77734f91fb9d
:CREATED:  [2026-08-07 Fri 19:40]
:BLOCKER:  ids(720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1)
:END:
*The most serious thing the 2026-08-07 interactive apply did, and the
mechanism is NOT yet understood.* Recorded with that uncertainty intact
rather than a plausible story, because a wrong explanation here is worse
than none.

*What is established, by inspection:*
- The queue contains exactly three =todo= events: =32272061 → DONE=
  (08:19), =720b2dcf → PLANNING= (11:51), =720b2dcf → DOING= (16:06).
  *None* names =feba67eb=.
- After the apply, =feba67eb= was =DONE=, carrying
  =- State "DONE" from "NEXT" [2026-08-07 Fri 08:19]= — stamped with
  *32272061's* event timestamp, and placed *outside* =:LOGBOOK:=, which
  is apply's signature under the =org-log-into-drawer= bug fixed the
  same day.
- =32272061=, the event's actual target, has *no* =DONE= state line at
  all — consistent with =org-todo "DONE"= being a no-op there, since it
  was already =DONE=.
- No trigger hook can produce this: the only =org-todo= calls in the
  trigger layer set ="DOING"=, ="TODO"= and ="NEXT"=. No heading carries
  a =:TRIGGER:= property, so =org-depend-trigger-todo= is not involved
  either.

*Two candidate mechanisms, neither confirmed:*
- =org-log-note-marker= surviving between items within a single apply.
  =org-store-log-note= is driven directly for each item; if a previous
  item's marker or =org-log-note-state=/=-previous-state= is still set
  when the next item runs, a note can be written at the wrong place with
  the wrong content. The leaked =post-command-hook= (fixed same day)
  makes this family of state-leak plausible.
- Mis-resolution in =claude-code-ide-org--at-id=, which uses
  =(org-id-find id 'marker)=. Less likely — =org-id-find-id-in-file=
  does a real property search rather than trusting a cached position —
  but not ruled out.

*Why this outranks everything else in the epic:* the failure is silent
and the output is plausible. A heading was marked complete, with a
correctly-formatted log line citing a real timestamp, and nothing
errored. That is precisely the "confidently wrong record"
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] exists to
eliminate — produced by the machinery built to eliminate it.

Repaired by hand: =feba67eb= restored to =TODO= (its state at commit
=c81cba6=, before the apply) and both spurious state lines removed.

*ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED 2026-08-07.* Reproduced deterministically, and it
was the first candidate above — stale global note state, though by a
more specific route than "leaks between items":

=org-todo= calls =org-add-log-setup= *only when the state actually
changes*. Applying a state item whose heading already holds the
requested state is a no-op that sets up no note at all. But
=org-log-note-marker=, =org-log-note-state= and
=org-log-note-previous-state= are *global* and persist from whatever
last touched them — including a different heading in a different file.
=--review-apply-state= then drove =org-store-log-note= unconditionally,
which faithfully wrote that stale note at that stale marker.

Which is exactly what the live symptoms said, once read carefully: the
=32272061 → DONE= event was a no-op (that heading was already =DONE=,
and indeed has no =DONE= log line to this day), so nothing was set up;
the marker still pointed at =feba67eb=, with state/previous-state left
as ="DONE"=/="NEXT"= — producing a correctly-formatted line, on the
wrong heading, citing a real timestamp, with no error anywhere.

*Fix:* clear =org-log-note-marker= immediately *before* calling
=org-todo=, so the post-call check answers "did *this* call set up a
note?" rather than "has anything, ever?". One line, but the reasoning
belongs in the comment, which it now has.

*Verified:* two new tests — the isolated mechanism (prime the marker at
a bystander heading in another file, apply a no-op transition, assert
the bystander is untouched), and the literal live shape (a batch whose
first item is a no-op followed by a real transition, asserting no
leakage between items and that the real one lands where it belongs).
Both fail against the previous code. =bin/test= 157/157.

*Not claimed:* that apply is now safe to run unattended against real
files. This was one bug of five found by a single interactive pass, and
[[id:f9f61c04-150b-4ee7-96c9-582cf2bda95a][f9f61c04]] — replaying state
events reality has moved past — is still open and still writes
plausible-looking wrong history.

*** DONE Apply must not replay a state event that reality has moved past :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       f9f61c04-150b-4ee7-96c9-582cf2bda95a
:CREATED:  [2026-08-07 Fri 16:06]
:BLOCKER:  ids(720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1)
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-10 Mon 11:22]--[2026-08-10 Mon 11:31] =>  0:09
- State "DONE"       from "DONE"       [2026-08-11 Tue 12:06] \\
  guard verified live, both branches
CLOCK: [2026-08-10 Mon 12:36]--[2026-08-10 Mon 12:45] =>  0:09
CLOCK: [2026-08-10 Mon 11:50]--[2026-08-10 Mon 12:00] =>  0:10
- [2026-08-10 Mon 11:22]--[2026-08-10 Mon 11:31] assigned at review
:END:

[[file:~/.claude/plans/temporal-wondering-sparrow.md][Plan]]

Found 2026-08-07 during the first interactive apply, and the most
consequential of the four bugs that run exposed — the other three were
mechanical, this one is a design gap.

*What happened:* the queue held a =todo → PLANNING= event stamped 11:51.
By the time it was applied, the heading had long since moved on to
=DOING= (promoted by =bin/hooks/exitplanmode-promote-planning=, which
drives elisp directly and so never enters the queue at all). Apply
replayed the stale event faithfully and *regressed the heading from
DOING back to PLANNING*, writing a =State "PLANNING" from "DOING"= line
to prove it. Nothing errored; the record simply became wrong in a
plausible-looking way.

*Why it is not just an =exitplanmode= problem:*
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]] already plans to
make that hook emit a queued =todo= event, which would have avoided this
particular instance. But the general hazard survives that fix — a queue
is reviewed at a moment of the human's choosing, possibly long after the
events were recorded, and *any* out-of-band change in between (a
hand-edit in Emacs, a different session, an org-agenda bulk action)
leaves the queued transition stale. The longer review is deferred, the
likelier this gets, and deferring review is the entire premise of the
design.

*Options, none settled:*
- *Verify before applying*: compare the event's implied "from" state
  against the heading's actual current state, and refuse — or flag in
  the review buffer — when they disagree. The queue does not currently
  record a "from" state, only a "to", so this needs a schema addition.
- *Show current state in the review buffer* next to the proposed one, so
  a human sees =DOING → PLANNING= and declines it themselves. Cheaper,
  and consistent with the design's "the human is the validation step"
  premise, but relies on them noticing.
- *Collapse per heading*: apply only the final queued state per heading
  rather than replaying each transition. Loses the intermediate log
  lines that [[file:~/.claude/plans/review-and-cutover.md][the plan]]
  deliberately wanted, and still regresses when reality moved past even
  the final one.

Leaning toward recording the "from" state at queue-append time and
surfacing a mismatch in the review buffer — cheap, no new failure mode,
and it makes the human's decision an informed one rather than a
guess. Not started.

*First-pass queue debt cleared 2026-08-10.* Before this heading's guard
could be built or tested, the pending queue had to reflect only genuinely
unapplied work. It did not: 62 events were pending and *none* were marked
applied -- no =.applied= watermark file existed at all, because the
2026-08-07 apply predates the fix that made the watermark an applied-*set*
rather than a high-water mark.

60 of those events belonged to session =d847eab1=, spanning
[2026-08-07 Fri 08:19] to [2026-08-08 Sat 09:11]. They were not pending
work, for two independent reasons:
- Their five =todo= events are the ones the 2026-08-07 apply already
  applied for real -- including the =32272061 -> DONE= no-op that caused
  [[id:3d93021d-1d8a-4657-bc5c-77734f91fb9d][3d93021d]], and the
  =720b2dcf -> PLANNING= that caused this heading.
- Their 51 =pause=/=resume= guideposts were recorded while live clocking
  was still running (the cutover has not happened; =session-pause= still
  calls =emacsclient= *and* appends), so the intervals they describe are
  already written in =:LOGBOOK:=. Applying them would have duplicated
  every one.

Moved to =~/.claude/org-updates/archive/=, not deleted -- it is the only
surviving record of what the first interactive pass was actually offered.
Verified the reader ignores it: =--queue-session-ids= calls
=directory-files= non-recursively, filtered to =\.jsonl\'=, so a
subdirectory cannot match.

Left pending on purpose: this session's own queue file, which is real
unapplied work and is what the second interactive pass will run against.

*Implemented 2026-08-10, both halves.* The leaning recorded above --
record the `from' state and surface the mismatch -- was taken, and both
were built rather than either alone: display without a recorded `from'
relies on the reader noticing, and a schema check without display
refuses silently.

*How `from' gets recorded, given the writer has no Emacs.*
=bin/hooks/queue-append= is bash and must stay runnable with no Emacs at
all -- that is the premise the queue rests on -- so it cannot ask what
state a heading held. Instead =org_set_todo= now reports it, as
=TODO state set to DOING (was NEXT): "HEADING"=, and the hook recovers
it with the same =sed= it already uses to recover =org_clock_out='s
heading =:ID:=. Authoritative in the same way: it cannot disagree with
what happened.

Three values, deliberately kept distinct, because collapsing any two
turns the check off silently:
- a state name -- comparable against reality;
- ="none"= -- the heading carried no keyword (org's nil);
- =null= -- an event queued before this field existed, meaning
  *unknown*, never *none*. These are not flagged: flagging every legacy
  event would only teach the reader to ignore the flag.

*What the review buffer does now.* A state line renders the whole
transition (=NEXT -> DOING=), not just its destination. When the
heading no longer holds the queued =from=, the line is prefixed =!= and
names what it actually holds. Marking such an item asks for confirmation
(=y-or-n-p=, safe here -- this is a real interactive command with a human
present, not the =emacsclient -e= path that hangs), and only a confirmed
item applies. =--review-apply-item= re-checks independently and refuses
otherwise, since an item can reach apply without passing the mark
gesture, and a heading can change after the buffer was drawn.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 164/164, =bin/queue-append-test= green. Six new
ERT tests plus four shell checks, all confirmed to *fail* against the
pre-change =config.el= (stashed and re-run, not assumed). The central
one reproduces the literal 2026-08-07 shape: a =NEXT -> PLANNING= event
applied against a heading now =DOING= is refused, the file is unchanged
byte for byte, no =State "PLANNING"= line appears -- and the same item
applies once explicitly confirmed.

*Not claimed:* the interactive pass. Everything above is batch, and
[[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]] established this
path behaves differently under a live Emacs. The by-hand run is
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s remaining gate,
and this heading stays =DOING= until it has happened.




*Sequencing knot, identified 2026-08-11.* This heading is
=:BLOCKER:=-ed by [[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]],
and its own outcome note above says it stays =DOING= until an interactive
pass verifies it. Those two cannot both be satisfied in the obvious
order: state items cannot be interactively verified before the cutover,
because =org_set_todo= currently writes live *and* queues, so every
queued =todo= event is stale against reality by construction and the
guard correctly refuses all of them.

It resolves only by accepting that this heading stays =DOING= across
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]] — or, cheaper and
better, by hand-writing a queue file whose =from= matches the heading's
actual state and applying that. That verifies the guard live today with
no cutover, and it is worth doing *before* the cutover rather than after,
since this guard is a large part of what makes the cutover safe to run.

*Verified live 2026-08-11, with a hand-written queue file.* The knot
recorded above — that this cannot be verified before the cutover,
because =org_set_todo= writes live *and* queues, so every queued event
is stale by construction — was routed around exactly as suggested:
a scratch org file, a scratch queue directory, two hand-written events,
and the real Emacs with the user's Doom config loaded. Nothing real was
touched and nothing was clocked.

Both branches, in the live image rather than under =emacs --batch=:
- =from= matching reality (=NEXT=, heading is =NEXT=): not flagged, no
  =!= prefix, applied, and it wrote
  =- State "DOING" from "NEXT" [2026-08-11 Tue 09:00]= — *backdated to
  the event's timestamp, not to now*. That is the specific claim
  [[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]] said batch could
  not settle, since native logging behaves differently outside a real
  interactive session.
- =from= disagreeing (=DOING=, heading is =DONE= — the literal shape of
  the 2026-08-11 batch): flagged stale, rendered with =!=, refused, and
  the file came back *byte-identical*.

*A false defect the first run produced, worth recording.* The matching
item applied but wrote no =State= line at all, which looked like a real
bug. It was the fixture: the scratch file's =#+TODO:= line omitted the
=!=/=@= logging markers the real files carry, and org correctly logs
nothing without them. Rerun with the real header, the line appeared. A
test fixture that differs from production in the one dimension under
test will manufacture exactly this kind of scare.

*And it settles what happened in the field the same day.* The user
applied a batch containing four already-realized transitions on
=b74e0f19= and =9d2fcdad=; the log now shows a =DONE -> DOING -> DONE=
round trip that never happened. Since the guard demonstrably fires, those
items were flagged and *confirmed* at the prompt — the override working
as designed. Two things follow, neither a bug: pre-cutover the review
buffer is full of items that are stale purely because the tool already
applied them live, and confirming a stale item writes plausible history
that is wrong. Both disappear at the cutover; until then, refusing is
almost always the right answer.
*** DONE Sort :LOGBOOK: chronologically, keeping notes with their continuations :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       af7d3687-6d1e-4480-a9e2-df772785713f
:CREATED:  [2026-08-07 Fri 16:06]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-12 Wed 17:01] \\
  template drawer is now a byte-identical fixed point
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-12 Wed 16:56] \\
  chronological LOGBOOK ordering
:END:

Requested 2026-08-07: a =:LOGBOOK:= sorted by start time is markedly
easier to read than the current arrangement, where
=--consolidate-logbook-text= emits every =CLOCK:= line newest-first and
then dumps every non-CLOCK line (state notes, guidepost annotations) in
a block at the end — so an annotation sits nowhere near the interval it
describes. =clock-template.org= already shows the intended shape:
entries reading chronologically top to bottom, each annotation beside
the =CLOCK:= line for the same span.

*Scope, as stated 2026-08-07:* one chronological ordering across *every*
entry style — =CLOCK:= lines, active and inactive range annotations, and
=State "X" from "Y"= transitions alike — keyed on start time. Not
per-style grouping that happens to be sorted within each group; a single
timeline. A state transition at 14:10 belongs between the interval that
ended at 14:00 and the one starting at 14:20, which is precisely what
makes the drawer legible as a narrative of the task.

*Why this is not a quick sort:* the drawer holds three different line
shapes, and one of them is multi-line. A =State "X" from "Y" [ts] \\=
line owns the indented continuation beneath it, and any reordering that
separates the two corrupts the note. This is exactly the failure
[[id:ba8249c1-28cd-4ff1-918b-4b8439345d9a][ba8249c1]] already fixed
once, where consolidation silently deleted non-CLOCK drawer lines — so
the risk here is demonstrated, not hypothetical.

Approach: extend =--parse-clock-lines= so each parsed entry carries its
sort key *and* its full text including continuations, rather than the
current split into =:closed= conses plus a flat =:other= list of
verbatim lines. Then sort by start time and re-emit. Note this changes
the newest-first convention org itself uses for =CLOCK:= lines; that is
the point of the request, but worth stating explicitly since org will
still *insert* new clocks at the top and the order will drift again
between consolidations.

Test against a drawer containing all three shapes at once, including a
note with a continuation between two clock lines, and assert nothing is
lost — the =ba8249c1= regression case is the one to extend.

*Outcome, 2026-08-12.* Done as specified, ascending, one timeline across
every entry style.

=--parse-clock-lines= now returns =:entries=, a list of
=(:time T :text TEXT)= in original order, replacing the old split into
=:closed= conses plus a flat =:other= list. A line opens a new entry when
it begins =CLOCK:= or =- =; every other non-blank line continues the
entry above it, which is what carries a =State ... \\= note's indented
continuation along with it. The sort key comes from one rule that covers
all four shapes, since in each the *first* timestamp is the right one —
=CLOCK:= interval start, state-note stamp, and range-annotation start,
active =<...>= and inactive =[...]= alike.

*The sort is stable, deliberately.* =clock-template.org= is not
internally consistent about ties: at 09:15 the annotation precedes its
=CLOCK:= line, at 13:30 it follows. So there is no tie rule to discover,
and inventing one would reshuffle drawers on every pass. A stable sort
keeps whatever adjacency already exists.

*Entries with no parseable timestamp keep their original order at the
end* — unplaceable, so not placed, but never dropped. That is the
=ba8249c1= lesson: position is cosmetic, survival is not.

*One thing fixed that only became visible here.*
=--format-clock-line= emitted =CLOCK:= lines unindented, which was
harmless while they were a single block, and visibly misaligning once
they interleave with notes that keep their indentation. Each entry now
keeps its own.

*Verified, and the strongest check is the cheapest one:* consolidating
=clock-template.org='s own drawer returns it *byte-identical*. The
documented target shape is a fixed point, not merely close to one — and
that fixture exercises four entry styles, two continuations and several
ties at once, richer than anything worth hand-writing, and it cannot
drift from the doc because it *is* the doc. =bin/test= 186 -> 189: that
fixed-point test, one asserting a note's continuation travels with it
past two clock lines that must move around it, and one covering tie
stability plus undated-line survival. The pre-existing
interval-preservation test was updated rather than deleted — it encoded
the old newest-first order, which is exactly what this task changes; its
real assertion (intervals unrounded and unmerged) is intact, and it now
also asserts a second pass is a no-op, so the new order cannot
oscillate.

*Accepted cost, restated because it is permanent:* org still *inserts*
new CLOCK lines newest-first, so a live drawer drifts back out of order
between consolidations. Nothing runs consolidation automatically now
that it is off the clock-out path, so in practice a drawer is sorted
when someone asks.

*** DONE Add a read-only MCP tool to inspect and summarize the pending queue :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       63a642c7-b04d-42dc-b6ec-3fb13df3ae04
:CREATED:  [2026-08-06 Thu 11:59]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-12 Wed 16:55] \\
  org_pending_updates shipped, 186/186
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-12 Wed 16:52] \\
  read-only pending-queue summary tool
:END:

Captured 2026-08-06 from conversation: nothing in the current tool
suite lets Claude (or the user, via conversation) ask "what's queued
but not yet applied?" without opening Emacs — a real, near-necessary
gap once =org_set_todo=/=org_clock_in=/=org_clock_out= stop mutating
the buffer immediately. A new read-only tool (name TBD, e.g.
=org_pending_updates=), listing queued events grouped by heading and
by session, with clear utility on its own merits independent of the
overlay concept below — this is the tool a human would consult before
deciding whether to run the review-and-apply command at all. Likely
shares its "read every =~/.claude/org-updates/*.jsonl=, join by
=:ID:=" logic with both the review-and-apply command above and the
overlay sub-task below; worth designing the parsing/grouping code
once and reusing it in all three rather than three separate
implementations.

*Outcome, 2026-08-12.* Shipped as =org_pending_updates=, read-only.
Takes an optional =session_id= to scope to one queue; returns items
grouped by heading, each rendered by the *same* =--review-describe= the
review buffer uses, over the *same* =--review-items-from-queue=, with
staleness judged exactly as the buffer judges it on open. The sharing
the heading asked for is literal rather than parallel: the summary and
the buffer cannot disagree about what is pending, because there is only
one implementation.

*The one design decision worth recording: it counts proposals, not queue
lines.* Measured the same day, =clock_in=/=clock_out= and unclustered
=pause=/=resume= guideposts are consumed by nothing — they exist to
attribute spans — so the files permanently hold far more lines than
there are items. Live against the real queue: *1 of 283* events backed
the single pending item. A tool that reported 283 would announce a
standing backlog that does not exist, which is the trap recorded on
[[id:78f485a8-e808-43ce-af5f-277a5c97c471][78f485a8]]. The output states
the ratio and names the remainder as scaffolding.

*A wrong number caught by running it, not by reading it.* The first
version reported "from 6 session(s)" — counting every session holding a
*residual* event rather than the sessions that contributed items. Only
one had. Corrected to derive the session set from the items' own backing
events, which is also what makes the ratio line honest.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 183 -> 186, three new tests — a queued transition
is summarized without touching the file and stays pending afterwards; a
lone =clock_in= yields "Nothing pending" while still reporting the event
count, so the two quantities cannot be conflated; and =session_id=
narrows the report while an empty string means "no scope" rather than a
session named "". The third also pins the never-signals contract every
other tool here holds. Live-reloaded and run against the real queue.
CLAUDE.md's MCP tool table gained a row — a factual addition, not part
of [[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]]'s rewrite,
which still owns the prose around it.

*** CANCELLED Design a read-time overlay for org_query/org_clock_report against the pending queue :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       c9292ce8-8fb5-41c0-8de1-bbf74ffa7fce
:CREATED:  [2026-08-06 Thu 11:59]
:END:

Captured 2026-08-06 from conversation. Motivating problem: once
=org_set_todo= only queues a transition, a heading Claude just "set
to DOING" still reads its old state to =org_query= (and everything
else) until a human runs the review pass — confusing mid-conversation
contradiction ("I set it to DOING" / query says otherwise) if left
unaddressed. Proposed shape, a pure read-time reconciliation with
*no* write side effect: at query time, read the =.org= file as it
stands today (unchanged), separately read the relevant queue file(s),
join by =:ID:=, and annotate/override the displayed state before
formatting the response. Three sub-decisions this needs, not just "go
merge them":
- **Whose queue?** Default to the *calling session's own* pending
  events — that's what actually prevents the within-one-conversation
  contradiction above. A lightweight "+N pending from other sessions"
  count on top is a smaller, separate call; avoid a full cross-session
  merge at query time, which would reintroduce exactly the kind of
  concurrent-state-guessing this whole redesign exists to get away
  from.
- **Multiple queued events for one heading.** Show the *latest* by
  timestamp, not the first — same "final proposed value wins" logic
  the apply command itself needs anyway.
- **=org_clock_report= is a different shape of problem than
  =org_query=** — a single overlaid *state* is easy; accumulated
  *duration* is not. Silently blending unconfirmed queued intervals
  into an authoritative-looking total risks the same "confidently
  wrong" failure flagged for the marker-desync case
  ([[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][53b0047d]]) — a queued
  interval might still get edited or rejected at review time. Keep
  applied and pending totals visibly separate ("applied: 2:15, +0:40
  pending review") rather than merged into one number.
- **Overlapping intervals on the same heading, once concurrent actors
  are possible** ([[id:16160d23-273b-4510-858e-db18508d04a0][16160d23]]
  makes this a real case, not hypothetical): *decided* 2026-08-06 —
  union, not sum, since two overlapping intervals mean the system was
  busy doing *something* during that span, not that twice the work
  happened. Explicit note: revisit the actual union algorithm's
  particulars (exact boundary handling, how it interacts with the
  applied-vs-pending separation above, whether a unioned span still
  cites which actor(s) contributed) once real queued messages exist
  to test it against, rather than finalizing the mechanics on
  hypothetical data now.

*Cancelled 2026-08-06*, same day it was captured, on review of the
sub-task set as a whole. It works against the premise of the refactor
that contains it: the whole point is that nothing presents unconfirmed
state as authoritative, and a read-time overlay reintroduces exactly
that — a synthesized disk+queue view no human has approved. The third
bullet above already concedes the hard half (durations must stay
*visibly separate*, not merged), which means this was never really an
overlay, just an annotation. Once the risky merge is stripped out, what
remains is "join queue data into =org_query= output" — a consumer of
[[id:63a642c7-b04d-42dc-b6ec-3fb13df3ae04][63a642c7]]'s parsing layer,
not a design task in its own right.

The motivating problem is real but has a cheaper fix that needs no new
machinery: =org_set_todo= returns /queued, not applied/, and Claude
believes its own tool result rather than re-reading the file to
confirm. Fold the surviving pieces into =63a642c7=: the "latest queued
event wins" rule (bullet 2) and the applied-vs-pending separation
(bullet 3) are both things that tool needs anyway. The union-not-sum
decision (bullet 4) belongs to
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s apply-time
reconciliation, which is where overlapping intervals actually have to
be resolved — it survives this cancellation, it just doesn't live here.

*** DONE Support per-subagent activity intervals via a compound (session_id, agent_id) key :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       16160d23-273b-4510-858e-db18508d04a0
:CREATED:  [2026-08-06 Thu 13:06]
:BLOCKER:  ids(feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0)
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "DONE"       from "MAYBE"      [2026-08-13 Thu 11:15] \\
  all three parts shipped; demonstrated end to end
:END:

Re-scoped =TODO= → =MAYBE= 2026-08-06, same day it was captured. Not a
retreat from the observation — the =agentId=-vs-hand-invented-=-bgN=
fidelity point below is correct and worth keeping on the record. It is
v2 scope on a design not yet validated, and it depends on
[[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]] resolving
favorably, hence the =:BLOCKER:=: a subagent's interval is *inherently*
retrospective by the time the orchestrator learns of it, so if org's
clock APIs turn out not to accept a backdated timestamp, there is no
mechanism for this sub-task to write through at all. Revisit once the
core queue + review loop actually exists and =3d576d29= has an answer.

Captured 2026-08-06 from conversation. A flat one-file/one-key-per-
Claude-Code-=session_id= scheme has no room for subagents — they
share their parent's =session_id= (confirmed from this project's own
background-planning batch transcripts: sidechain events carry the
orchestrating session's =sessionId=, distinguished only by a separate
=agentId= field visible to the orchestrator's own task-completion
metadata). =org_log_background_plan= already worked around this with
a hand-invented =<session-id>-bgN= label — but that throws away the
*real* =agentId= the system already assigns (confirmed present in
this session's own tool results, e.g. =a4bb098d74a939dc9=) in favor
of an arbitrary per-batch number. Fix: use the real =(session_id,
agent_id)= pair as a genuine compound key on each queued event,
rather than reinventing a synthetic label.

**File layout**: one file per real =session_id= (matching one writer
stream, no contention), with =agent_id= as a field on individual
events, not a separate file per subagent — a subagent's contribution
is typically 1-2 events, not enough to justify file proliferation.
The review-and-apply command's existing "group by heading and
session" logic extends naturally to sub-group by =agent_id= when
present.

**Confirmed baseline, no open uncertainty**: the orchestrating
session writes these events *on the subagent's behalf* after each
one completes — same shape as today's =org_log_background_plan= call,
just with the real =agent_id= and a genuine start/end interval
instead of one timestamp, computed from the completion notification's
own =duration_ms= field (already present in every background-agent
completion this project has seen). Requires no change to what
subagents are permitted to do.

**Why this data gets auto-derived treatment while human-session data
doesn't** (see
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s core design
principle): a subagent interval carries none of the "was the human
actually attending, or idle/elsewhere" ambiguity that makes human-
session reconstruction a guidepost-only, human-driven exercise — a
subagent was mechanically either running or not. That's precisely
what makes =duration_ms= trustworthy enough to write directly, no
manual reconstruction needed.

**Separate, genuinely uncertain stretch goal, do not assume either
way**: whether a subagent could instead self-report its own event
mid-flight (more accurate than reconstructing from =duration_ms=
after the fact) depends on whether a spawned subagent process has
access to its own =agent_id= from inside its own execution context —
unknown, not verified. If confirmed, this would also mean the
"subagents never call mutating tools" rule could relax specifically
for queue-appends (now safe, since appending touches no shared live
state, unlike the old live-clock-mutation risk that originally
justified the rule) — but the compound-key fidelity gain does not
depend on resolving this; the orchestrator-writes-on-behalf-of
baseline gets it in full on its own.

Directly informs
[[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]] (backdated-
timestamp support): a subagent's interval is *inherently*
retrospective by the time the orchestrator learns of it, giving that
sub-task a second, concrete, non-hypothetical reason it matters,
beyond the general human-review-lag case.

*CORRECTION 2026-08-07 — the "confirmed baseline" above is wrong, and
the "genuinely uncertain stretch goal" is resolved in the affirmative.*
Both were reasoned from this project's own transcripts rather than from
the hook documentation, which says plainly: tool events such as
=PreToolUse= and =PostToolUse= fire the same configured hooks inside a
subagent as in the main conversation, and the payload carries
=agent_id= (unique per subagent call, absent on the main thread) and
=agent_type= (e.g. ="Explore"=). There is also a distinct
=SubagentStop= event.

Consequences, in descending order of how much they change this
sub-task:
- *No orchestrator-side reconstruction is needed at all.* The subagent's
  own tool calls are already attributed, in real time. The whole
  "orchestrator writes on the subagent's behalf, computing the interval
  from =duration_ms=" design is unnecessary — and it was the *only*
  thing making a subagent's interval inherently retrospective, so the
  paragraph above overstates this sub-task's dependency on backdating.
- *The =-bgN= synthetic label problem is already solved*, not merely
  solvable. =bin/hooks/queue-append= reads =$payload.agent_id= and has
  since it was written; =agent_type= was added 2026-08-07 alongside this
  correction. Verified end-to-end: a synthetic subagent payload produces
  a queue line carrying both fields, and a main-thread payload produces
  nulls. So the compound key exists *today*, with no code left to write
  for the attribution half.
- *What actually remains* is therefore much smaller than this heading
  describes: deciding whether the "subagents never call mutating tools"
  rule relaxes for queue-appends. Pre-cutover it must *not* — the tools
  still mutate the live global clock, so concurrent subagents calling
  =org_clock_in= would fight over it, which is the exact failure
  [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] exists to
  remove. Post-[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]] a
  tool call is a pure append and the hazard is gone by construction.
  That, not attribution, is the real gate.

Left =MAYBE= rather than promoted: the remaining decision is genuinely
blocked on the cutover, and nothing is gained by settling it earlier.
The =:BLOCKER:= on =3d576d29= is now stale — that heading is =DONE= —
but the practical dependency has moved to =feba67eb=, so it is
retargeted accordingly.

*Resolved 2026-08-13, and every remaining piece turned out to be
already done — two of them without anyone noticing they belonged here.*
Reconciled against
[[id:0d789b68-faca-4911-868a-fdd70acbfd40][0d789b68]] at the user's
request. Taking this heading's three parts in turn:

1. *The compound key itself* — settled 2026-08-07, as the correction
   above records: =bin/hooks/queue-append= reads =$payload.agent_id= and
   =agent_type= straight from the hook payload.
2. *Sub-grouping by =agent_id= in the review command* — this heading's
   "File layout" paragraph predicted the review logic "extends naturally
   to sub-group by =agent_id= when present." It did not, until
   =0d789b68=: attribution walked each *session's* stream with a single
   =current=, so concurrent subagents clobbered each other. Fixed
   2026-08-13 by keying that walk per lane. So the piece this heading
   assumed would be free was the one that actually needed writing.
3. *The gate — whether "subagents never call mutating tools" relaxes for
   queue-appends.* The correction above pinned this on
   [[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]], which is
   =DONE=, and reasoned the hazard is "gone by construction" once a tool
   call is a pure append. Exercised deliberately on 2026-08-12: two
   concurrent subagents were permitted =org_clock_in=/=org_clock_out=
   and nothing contended, because those tools are validators that write
   no buffer. Verified first, per this project's habit — all three are
   pure appends with zero =save-buffer= calls in their bodies.

*The end-to-end evidence this heading was actually asking for:* on
2026-08-12 two subagents' intervals reached =:LOGBOOK:= on their own
headings, each attributed to its own =agent_id=, rendered with
*inactive* timestamps so they stay out of =org-agenda= — which is
exactly "per-subagent activity intervals via a compound =(session_id,
agent_id)= key," working.

The stretch goal is resolved affirmative too, and demonstrated rather
than reasoned: a subagent self-reports mid-flight, since the hook fires
inside its own execution context with its own =agent_id=. No
orchestrator-side reconstruction from =duration_ms= exists or is needed.

*One thing this does not close:* the relaxed rule is now demonstrated
but *undocumented*. CLAUDE.md and the org skill still carry no guidance
on what a subagent may call, and the old blanket prohibition survives
only as prose in this heading. That belongs to
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]]'s rewrite —
recorded here so it is not lost when this heading is archived.

*** DONE Verify org's clock/logging APIs actually support backdated timestamps for batch apply-time backfill :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad
:CREATED:  [2026-08-06 Thu 10:47]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-06 Thu 22:35]--[2026-08-06 Thu 22:40] =>  0:05
:END:

The explicitly-flagged load-bearing assumption from the parent
heading, worth its own sub-task precisely so it isn't skipped or
assumed true by default. Evaluate early, before the rest of the
design is trusted — this could reshape or invalidate parts of the
review-and-apply command's design above.

*Clock half: confirmed 2026-08-06*, read directly out of the live
straight build (=~/.config/emacs/.local/straight/build-29.4/org/
org-clock.el=), not from memory or docs:

: (defun org-clock-in  (&optional select start-time))      ; line 1380
: (defun org-clock-out (&optional switch-to-state fail-quietly at-time)) ; line 1808

=START-TIME= flows straight into =org-clock-start-time=, which is what
=org-insert-timestamp= writes as the CLOCK timestamp (line ~1543) — so
this is genuine backdating, not a rounding or display argument. Backfilling
a completed historical interval is therefore a matched =org-clock-in= /
=org-clock-out= pair with both endpoints supplied up front. Note what
this implies about *mode*: native clocking gets used as a writer of
already-closed intervals, never as a live stopwatch, which is what
removes the open-ended shared window every desync incident traces to.

*Executed 2026-08-06 22:26-22:45. Gate result: BOTH HALVES PASS.*
Verified by running org 9.8.7 (the straight build Doom actually uses)
against scratch files, not by reading docs. Scripts:
=$CLAUDE_JOB_DIR/tmp/wave0-verify{,-b}.el=.

*A. Clock backdating — CONFIRMED, end to end.* =(org-clock-in nil
START)= then =(org-clock-out nil nil END)=, both non-interactive,
produced exactly:

: CLOCK: [2026-08-06 Thu 19:39]--[2026-08-06 Thu 20:39] =>  1:00

Correct interval, correct duration arithmetic, correct =:LOGBOOK:=
placement, no clock left running afterwards. A completed historical
interval is therefore a matched pair with both endpoints supplied up
front — native clocking used as a writer of already-closed intervals,
never as a live stopwatch.

*B. Logging backdating — CONFIRMED, but NOT by the mechanism the plan
assumed.* The plan's Phase 0 hypothesis — let-bind
=org-log-note-effective-time= around =org-todo= — *does not work*.
=org-add-log-setup= (=org.el:11031=) =setq=s that variable from
=(org-current-effective-time)=, clobbering any caller's =let=. What
does work is shadowing the *function*:

: (cl-letf (((symbol-function 'org-current-effective-time)
:            (lambda () past)))
:   (org-todo "DOING"))

Result, against a control run with no shadowing (=now= was 22:41):

: - State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-06 Thu 17:41]   ; shadowed
: - State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-06 Thu 22:41]   ; control

Three mechanics the apply command will need, learned the hard way:
- Even a =!= (timestamp-only) keyword *defers* through
  =post-command-hook= → =org-add-log-note=. Confirms config.el's field
  note at =org_set_todo=. =org-log-note-how= is =time= for =!=, which
  takes =org-store-log-note='s no-prompt branch — but =org-add-log-note=
  unconditionally =pop-to-buffer=s =*Org Note*= *before* testing that,
  which is the non-interactive breakage.
- The deferred path can be driven directly, bypassing
  =post-command-hook= entirely: call =org-store-log-note= with an empty
  =*Org Note*= buffer current. It does =(buffer-string)= + =(kill-buffer)=
  on the current buffer, so the current buffer must be the note buffer,
  not the org file.
- =org-store-log-note= ends with =set-window-configuration=, which
  errors if =org-log-note-window-configuration= is nil. The note is
  already written by then, but bind it anyway.

*Live-environment caveat, and a real interaction found:* re-running B
through =emacsclient -e= against the live Doom Emacs produced the clock
line but *no state-change log line at all*. The difference is
=claude-code-ide-org--trigger-auto-clock-in=, which is live on
=org-trigger-hook= and fires during =org-todo=; its =org-clock-in= runs
while the state-change note is still pending and the note never lands.
Not fully root-caused, but the observation is solid and it matters for
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]: the apply
command cannot have a trigger hook clocking in underneath it while a
log note is outstanding. Sequencing state and clock, or suppressing the
trigger during apply, is now a design requirement, not a nicety.

(Incidental: CLAUDE.md says =org-trigger-hook=/=org-blocker-hook=
enforcement is "not built yet." It is — four functions are live on
=org-trigger-hook=. Fold that correction into
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]].)

*** DONE Survey org's own API for built-in mechanisms we may have overlooked or dismissed too quickly :code:
:PROPERTIES:

:CREATED:  [2026-08-06 Thu 10:47]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-14 Fri 13:32] \\
  surveying org-element for drawer writes
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-14 Fri 13:36] \\
  all four checklist items discharged
:END:

[[file:~/.claude/plans/temporal-wondering-sparrow.md][Plan]]

Explicit instruction for whoever evaluates this: do this *before*
committing to bespoke replacements for anything above. This project
has, on more than one occasion, built custom machinery
(=claude-code-ide-org--append-to-drawer=, the hand-rolled
=:SESSIONS:= parser/consolidator, the ownership-variable tracking
this refactor now proposes deleting) without first exhausting what
org itself already offers. Assiduously check, at minimum: whether
=org-clock-in=/=org-clock-out= have documented or de facto support
for an explicit non-"now" timestamp; the actual signature and
batch-friendliness of =org-add-log-note=/=org-store-log-note= outside
the interactive =post-command-hook= path; whether org's own
=org-element=/structural-editing API (as opposed to this project's
current regex/position-based drawer editing) would make the apply
command's drawer writes more robust for free; and whether
=org-agenda-bulk-*= (the existing precedent this design's review UI
is modeled on informally) has machinery worth calling directly rather
than reimplementing. Do not treat this project's own prior choices in
this area as proof no better native mechanism exists — several of
them were made under time pressure chasing a live bug, not after a
deliberate API survey.

*Scope narrowed 2026-08-06*: the first item on that checklist — whether
=org-clock-in=/=org-clock-out= take an explicit non-"now" timestamp —
is answered, and duplicated
[[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]] outright. Both
take one (=START-TIME= / =AT-TIME=); see that heading for the confirmed
signatures. Drop it from this survey and treat =3d576d29= as the owner
of the timestamp question, so the load-bearing bit isn't buried inside
a broad sweep — burying it is precisely how it gets skipped, which is
why it was broken out separately in the first place. The remaining
three checklist items (=org-add-log-note= batch-friendliness,
=org-element= vs. this project's regex/position drawer editing,
=org-agenda-bulk-*=) are untouched and still the substance here.

*Agenda lead — CONFIRMED 2026-08-06, exactly as hypothesized.* Ran the
cheap check this heading specifies: a scratch heading with one active
and one inactive list item inside =:LOGBOOK:=, plus a =CLOCK:= line,
then =org-agenda-list=. Only the active one appeared:

: agenda:     10:00...... Guidepost host      ; <2026-08-06 Thu 10:00>

The inactive item (=[... 11:00]=) and the =CLOCK:= line were both
absent. So an active timestamp inside a =:LOGBOOK:= drawer *does* reach
the agenda — this is the working mechanism for
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s "guidepost"
annotations, and org's own agenda does become a free retrospective
"what did I actually attend to" view with no bespoke tooling. Verified
in org 9.8.7, the straight build Doom uses.

**One concrete lead already found, added 2026-08-06 — verify, don't
rediscover from scratch**: active (=<...>=) timestamps are picked up
by =org-agenda=/=org-agenda-list=, inactive (=[...]=) ones are not,
including (per informal web research, not yet confirmed live in this
project's own Emacs) freeform list-item notes inside a =:LOGBOOK:=
drawer, not just top-level buffer text. This is indirectly consistent
with existing evidence already in this codebase: =CLOCK:= lines
deliberately use inactive timestamps specifically to stay out of the
agenda, which only makes sense if the reverse also holds. If
confirmed, this becomes the mechanism behind
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s active-marker
"guidepost" annotations — org's own agenda would double as a free,
retrospective "what did I actually attend to" view, no bespoke
tooling needed for that specific piece. **Verify before relying on
it**: on a scratch heading, put one active-timestamp and one
inactive-timestamp list item inside =:LOGBOOK:=, run
=org-agenda-list=, confirm exactly the active one appears — cheap,
direct, matches this project's own "confirmed live, not just
theorized" discipline.


*Further discharged than its state suggests, audited 2026-08-11.* Two of
this heading's four checklist items are already answered, in writing,
elsewhere:
- *Backdated timestamps* — settled by
  [[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]], which also
  answered the =org-add-log-note=/=org-store-log-note= batch-driving
  question in the course of proving apply could work at all.
- *=org-agenda-bulk-*= as a callable base* — answered in
  =review-and-cutover.md=, with a real finding rather than a shrug:
  =org-agenda-bulk-mark= keys off =org-hd-marker= text properties on
  agenda-formatted lines and navigates by property change, so it only
  works in a buffer org-agenda itself built. Its *design* was copied; its
  code could not be.

What genuinely remains is the =org-element= question — whether org's
structural editing API would make apply's drawer writes more robust than
the current regex and position arithmetic. That one has *gained* weight:
every drawer-level data-loss incident this project has had
(=ba8249c1=, =b74e0f19=) lived in exactly that code.

*Answered 2026-08-14, and the answer inverts the expectation above:
migrate nothing.* Probed live against org 9.8.7 rather than reasoned
about, on the five drawer shapes the real files actually contain.

*First, the premise was already half wrong.* The *writer*,
=claude-code-ide-org--append-to-drawer-1=, does not do position
arithmetic at all — it validates each candidate with
=org-element-type-p ... 'drawer= and inserts at
=org-element-contents-end=. The org-element migration this heading
imagines was already made for the write path, some time ago and without
being recorded here. What uses a regex is the *reader*,
=claude-code-ide-org--drawer-content-bounds=, and only for its tail: it
finds the =:END:= line with =^[ \t]*:END:[ \t]*$= instead of asking the
element for =contents-end=.

*Second, that regex tail is not the liability it looks like.* Measured,
=(:regex ...)= against =(:element ...)=, on four shapes — a normal
drawer, one following a =:PROPERTIES:= drawer, an indented drawer, and a
=:LOGBOOK:= whose note continuation is the literal text =:END:= — the
two agree *exactly*, character position for character position. The
fourth is the one worth naming: a continuation line of =:END:= ends the
drawer early, and it does so in =org-element= too, because that is
org's own grammar rather than this code's approximation of it.

*Third, and decisive: org-element is strictly worse for the empty
drawer.* An empty =:LOGBOOK:=/=:END:= pair yields
=(nil nil)= from =org-element-contents-begin=/=-end=, where the regex
version yields =(20 20)= — a valid, empty region that callers can do
arithmetic on. A naive migration would hand =nil= to every caller of
this function for a case that occurs whenever a drawer has just been
emptied. So the current hybrid — org-element to *validate*, regex to
*delimit* — is a correct design rather than an unmigrated legacy, and
should be left alone. That is the survey's answer to its last open
checklist item.

*What the probe turned up on the way, which is the real yield:* the
reader and the writer disagree about a decoy. Given a heading whose body
contains a =:LOGBOOK:=-shaped line inside =#+begin_example= *before* the
real drawer, the writer loops past it and appends correctly, while the
reader checks only its first match, finds it is not a drawer, and
returns =nil= — reporting "this heading has no =:LOGBOOK:=" when it has
one. Filed as
[[id:f42641ab-fdd6-4754-add9-490dd6b24ef8][f42641ab]]; not fixed here,
because this heading's job is to survey rather than to build.

*Every checklist item is now discharged*, three of them elsewhere
(=3d576d29= for backdated timestamps and =org-add-log-note=,
=review-and-cutover.md= for =org-agenda-bulk-*=) and the last one here.
The standing instruction that opens this heading — check org first,
because this project has repeatedly built bespoke machinery without
doing so — held up: it did not overturn the architecture, but it did
find one real defect and it did stop a migration that would have
introduced =nil= bounds into working code.

:END:

- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-14 Fri 14:06] \\
