- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-14 Fri 14:09] \\
  shared helper shipped, mutations checked
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 14:06]--[2026-08-14 Fri 14:09] =>  0:03
- [2026-08-14 Fri 14:06]--[2026-08-14 Fri 14:09] reallocated from e51d6ba1's mis-suggested span
:END:

*Found 2026-08-14 by [[id:c084553c-0621-4a96-9fa1-f32850aeec6a][c084553c]]'s
API probe, as a side effect rather than as its object.* The two halves
of the drawer code disagree about what to do when a =:LOGBOOK:=-shaped
line is not really a drawer.

=claude-code-ide-org--append-to-drawer-1= searches in a =while= loop:
each candidate is checked with =org-element-type-p ... 'drawer=, and a
line that fails the check is stepped past in favour of the next.
=claude-code-ide-org--drawer-content-bounds= runs the same
=re-search-forward= *once*, applies the same check, and on failure
returns =nil= — which its callers read as "this heading has no
=:LOGBOOK:= drawer."

*Demonstrated, not inferred.* A heading whose body holds a
=:LOGBOOK:=/=:END:= pair inside =#+begin_example= *before* its real
drawer:

- the writer appended correctly, to the real drawer, ignoring the decoy;
- the reader returned =nil=.

*Why it matters, and why it is filed under this epic:* the reader is
what the whole-drawer rewrite path uses — sorting and consolidation,
=af7d3687='s and =12e0adac='s code. A false "no drawer here" means such
a pass silently skips a heading that has one. Silent skipping is the
same failure family as =ba8249c1= and =b74e0f19=, the two drawer
data-loss incidents, which is exactly why =c084553c= was pointed at this
code in the first place.

*Reachability is low, and worth stating honestly so this is not
over-prioritised.* The decoy must sit between the heading line and the
real drawer, and the standard layout puts =:LOGBOOK:= immediately after
=:PROPERTIES:=, leaving no room. It becomes reachable when body prose
lands *above* the drawer — which is not hypothetical: that is precisely
the malformation corrected on
[[id:b5f94b88-76e3-4a3c-ac73-729f34d33691][b5f94b88]] on 2026-08-14, a
heading whose "Promoted out of" paragraph had been sitting between the
two drawers.

*The fix is to make the reader loop like the writer* — same predicate,
same stepping — and the more durable answer is that these two should
share one find-the-drawer helper rather than each carrying its own
search. Worth a test built from the probe's own decoy fixture, which is
already written.

*Outcome, 2026-08-14. There were three copies of the search, not two.*
=claude-code-ide-org--drawer-contains-line-p= carried a third, and it
looped correctly — so the split was two right to one wrong, which is
exactly the argument for a shared helper rather than a one-line patch to
the odd one out. All three now call
=claude-code-ide-org--find-drawer=, which returns the *element* rather
than positions, because the three callers want different things from it:
an insertion point, a content region, and a line scan.

*The empty-drawer fallback is now enforced rather than merely
documented.* [[id:c084553c-0621-4a96-9fa1-f32850aeec6a][c084553c]]
found that =org-element-contents-begin=/=-end= are =nil= for an empty
drawer and concluded the naive migration would be a regression;
=--drawer-content-bounds= keeps returning an empty-but-valid region, and
a test now fails if anyone makes that migration anyway. The survey's
finding has a guard instead of a paragraph.

*Verified.* Five tests, 255 green, and both mutations behaved exactly as
predicted rather than merely "a test failed": reverting the shared search
to its old single-shot form failed all four decoy tests and left the
empty-drawer test passing, while performing the naive =org-element=
migration failed *only* the empty-drawer test. Complementary, so the two
properties are independently pinned. Then reloaded live and run against
the real =b5f7c5c7= heading, whose =:LOGBOOK:= it reads as a 312-
character region.

*Reachability is unchanged and still low* — the decoy must precede the
real drawer — but the cost of the fix was one helper and the removal of
two duplicated searches, so the argument for waiting for a live incident
was weak.

*** DONE Decide the fate of the stale-interval recovery feature, whose premise this refactor removes :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c
:CREATED:  [2026-08-06 Thu 22:26]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-12 Wed 17:40]--[2026-08-12 Wed 17:42] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-12 Wed 17:40]--[2026-08-12 Wed 17:42] subagent investigating recovery fate
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-14 Fri 13:40] \\
  deciding the recovery feature's fate
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-14 Fri 13:45] \\
  decided and executed: guess retired
:END:

Captured 2026-08-06 from conversation. The whole stale-interval
recovery subsystem — =SessionStart= hook →
=bin/hooks/session-start-recovery-check= →
=claude-code-ide-org-write-session-start-report=,
=claude-code-ide-org-close-open-interval=, the
=claude-code-ide-org-session-recovery-enabled= and
=claude-code-ide-org-working-hours= defcustoms, and the
working-hours-based guess heuristic — exists for exactly one scenario:
a crash or shutdown killing Emacs while a =CLOCK:= line or =:SESSIONS:=
=Resumed= entry is still *open*, leaving it open indefinitely.

Under the queue+review model that scenario stops being reachable for
=CLOCK:= lines. The apply command knows both endpoints before it writes
anything (=org-clock-in= with =START-TIME=, =org-clock-out= with
=AT-TIME=, back to back, single actor) — intervals are written already
closed, so there is no window in which a crash can strand one. A crash
mid-apply is a different and much narrower failure than the one this
feature was built for.

Genuinely undecided, do not assume the answer is "delete it":
- =:SESSIONS:= =Resumed= entries may still strand, depending on what
  [[id:32272061-1d78-4726-b13b-90338edb2ba5][32272061]] decides about
  whether pause/resume stay paired drawer entries or become bare
  append-only queue events. If they become queue events, the =pause=
  simply never arrives and the queue is inert — nothing to recover.
- The =SessionStart= report slot itself may be worth *repurposing*
  rather than removing: "you have N unreviewed updates pending" at the
  start of a day is arguably more useful than today's stale-interval
  question, and is the same surface
  [[id:290b6fc5-3ebb-4827-9a97-5a3542e354b6][290b6fc5]] and
  [[id:63a642c7-b04d-42dc-b6ec-3fb13df3ae04][63a642c7]] are circling
  from the statusline and tool sides. Three things wanting the same
  data is a signal to design the read path once.
- The =pmset -g log= lead recorded in CLAUDE.md ("Not yet attempted")
  should be closed out as *won't-do* rather than left dangling if the
  guess heuristic goes away.

Sequence after [[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]
exists and works — retiring a safety net before its replacement is
proven is how you lose data you didn't know you were relying on.

*Evidence against the working-hours half specifically, 2026-08-13.* The
gap survey at
[[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]] measured 422 queue
events and found the premise =claude-code-ide-org-working-hours= rests
on — that absence is predictable from the clock — does not hold on this
project's own data. Of 19 human gaps over 2000s, *11 begin inside
working hours* (07:00, 10:00, 12:00, 14:00, 15:00, 16:00, 17:00),
including the two longest at 151 and 158 minutes, starting 12:59 and
15:34. Daytime absence is indistinguishable from nighttime absence.

So =--guess-stop-time='s "end of working hours on the day the interval
opened" is not a weak signal, it is close to no signal: it would have
guessed wrong for the majority of the long gaps actually observed. What
*does* separate attending from absent is gap *length*, cleanly and with
an empty band between the modes — which is a property of the queue
stream, not of the clock.

This strengthens the case for retiring =--guess-stop-time= and
=claude-code-ide-org-working-hours= outright, which is what the second
independent review of this heading recommended, rather than keeping them
as a fallback. It does *not* touch
=claude-code-ide-org-close-open-interval=, the repair function, whose
case rests on
[[id:53b0047d-c55c-486c-8eed-ba4994d97a1a][53b0047d]] and a human's own
=C-c C-x C-i= remaining reachable.

*Decided and executed 2026-08-14. Split verdict: keep the detector and
the repair, retire the guess.*

*First, this heading's own premise is wrong, and that is what saved the
feature.* It states that under queue+review the crash-strands-an-open-
CLOCK scenario "stops being reachable for =CLOCK:= lines," because apply
writes intervals already closed. Apply does — but apply is not the only
writer. =claude-code-ide-org--trigger-auto-clock-in= still fires on any
hand-edit to =DOING=/=PLANNING= and opens a *live, unclosed* clock, which
is asserted directly by the suite (=config-test.el:611= checks the buffer
holds =CLOCK: [= after a bare =org-todo=) and which happened in practice
on 2026-08-12, when the toggle described in
[[id:ab75d6d2-0e59-405d-92c6-2c67488db133][ab75d6d2]] minted an interval
that had to be deleted by hand. The Doom config's =kill-emacs-hook= and
=suspend-hook= close such a clock on a *clean* exit; a crash does not.
So the scenario is reached through a narrower door than before — a human
at a buffer rather than a session — but it is the same room, and
retiring the detector would have removed a net that still catches
something. Verified quiet, not merely assumed: the detector reports 0
findings against the real files today, and neither tracked file contains
an open =CLOCK:= line.

*Second, the guess is gone,* and this part was already argued twice
independently. =--guess-stop-time= and
=claude-code-ide-org-working-hours= are deleted outright rather than kept
as a weak fallback. [[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]]
measured the premise and it does not hold: 11 of 19 long gaps begin
inside working hours, including the two longest. The deciding argument
against a *weaker* fallback rather than none is
[[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]]'s finding, measured
on this project's own review buffer — a plausible suggestion is harder to
reject than none, so a guess that is wrong for most observed gaps is
worse than an honest question. The report now states the open timestamp,
asks what time work actually stopped, and explicitly instructs the
relaying session not to invent one.

*Third, the two branches this heading left open are both closed by
events rather than by argument.* The =:SESSIONS:= branch is moot:
[[id:9d2fcdad-9bf7-47b6-8018-223b13ec4577][9d2fcdad]] retired the drawer
and deleted every instance, so a =Resumed= entry can no longer strand.
And the =pmset -g log= lead is closed as *won't-do* in CLAUDE.md, since
it existed to make a better guess and there is no longer a guess to
improve — distinguished there from
[[id:1a5a5254-f62d-46e7-9a6c-3d84f2f52873][1a5a5254]], which wants power
assertions for *review-time attribution* and is untouched by this.

*Deliberately not done: repurposing the =SessionStart= slot.* The
"you have N unreviewed updates pending" idea stays where it was filed.
There are already two =SessionStart= hooks wired in =.claude/settings.json=
(=session-start-recovery-check= and =.claude/hooks/session-context.sh=),
so nothing about keeping this one blocks that one; and the want is shared
with [[id:290b6fc5-3ebb-4827-9a97-5a3542e354b6][290b6fc5]] and
[[id:63a642c7-b04d-42dc-b6ec-3fb13df3ae04][63a642c7]], which is an
argument for designing that read path once, not for smuggling it in here.

*Verified.* 250 tests green. The two =--guess-stop-time= tests were
deleted with the function — they asserted the guess was *computed*
correctly, which it always was; what failed was the premise. They are
replaced by one test asserting the property that now matters: the report
states exactly one timestamp and tells the reader not to propose
another. Confirmed to discriminate by re-introducing a second, later
timestamp into the report, which failed it. Then reloaded live, with
=fmakunbound=/=makunbound= for the deleted symbols per the org-dev
skill. The caller-reference check returned *non-nil* for both callers —
inspected rather than believed, and both matches are inside docstrings
written about the retirement itself, which is precisely the false
positive that skill warns about. The batch suite is the independent
proof: it loads =config.el= into a fresh Emacs where those symbols never
existed at all.

*One documentation drift fixed here, one recorded for
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]].* CLAUDE.md claimed
both recovery =defcustom=s were "set in =~/.config/doom/config.el=";
neither is, and both have always run at their defaults — corrected while
removing the retired one. Separately, CLAUDE.md still shows
=(add-hook 'kill-emacs-hook #'org-clock-out)= where the live config
actually installs =claude-code-ide-org--clock-out-if-clocking=. That one
is left for the rewrite, since it is not this heading's to fix, but it is
load-bearing for the argument above about clean exits.

*** DONE Decide whether the :SESSIONS: drawer and its machinery survive the refactor :code:

  user decided: delete SESSIONS entirely
CLOCK: [2026-08-11 Tue 11:33]--[2026-08-11 Tue 11:38] =>  0:05
:END:

Captured 2026-08-07 from conversation, prompted by asking a narrow
question — is =claude-code-ide-org-clock-out= still useful once this is
over? — that turned out to have a much wider answer. Deliberately
*deferred*: this is codebase simplification, best done once the dust has
settled and it is clear what can actually be jettisoned, not decided
speculatively now. Hence =MAYBE= and the =:BLOCKER:= on
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]].

*What is potentially dead, with call counts as of 2026-08-07:*
- =claude-code-ide-org-clock-out= — exactly *one* caller
  (=claude-code-ide-org-session-pause=, config.el:471), which
  =feba67eb= removes. Nothing else in the module, the hooks, or the
  user's Doom config calls it; the Doom hooks use raw =org-clock-out=.
- =claude-code-ide-org-consolidate-history= — two callers, both likely
  to go: inside the wrapper above (config.el:438), and inside
  =close-open-interval= (config.el:901), which belongs to the recovery
  feature [[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]]
  questions the premise of. Not an MCP tool, so no external caller.
- With it, the =:SESSIONS:= text machinery it exists to drive:
  =--parse-session-lines=, =--format-session-line=,
  =--consolidate-sessions-text= (config.el:1045-1078).
- And plausibly the =:SESSIONS:= *drawer itself*.

*The case that the drawer is now redundant:* CLAUDE.md describes it as
"the bracketing history: a plain timestamped log of every pause and
resume, so the full wall-clock arc of the task — including the gaps —
stays visible." Under the new model that is exactly what the guidepost
annotations in =:LOGBOOK:= do, derived from the same =pause=/=resume=
events. Supporting, though neither is decisive: =clock-template.org=,
the worked example defining the target output, contains *zero* mentions
of =:SESSIONS:= — but its own legend scopes it to ":LOGBOOK: conventions",
so that silence may be scope rather than deletion. And after the cutover
the only remaining writer would be =org_log_background_plan=, appending
a single ="Background-planned"= entry — thin justification for a whole
drawer plus parser plus consolidator.

*The case against, which deserves real weight:* CLAUDE.md presents the
two mechanisms as deliberately and co-equally separate, not as an
accident to be tidied away, and that separation was a considered
decision rather than drift. Retiring it is a reversal, so it should be
argued on its merits rather than justified by "nothing calls it any
more."

*Do not lose these three things* — they live inside the wrapper today
and are still needed by
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s apply step:
=org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks= bound nil (or a short confirmed
interval is dropped at the org level before it can be written); the
buffer save; and =--log-source= attribution. That last one is the
easiest to lose silently — =--log-event= defaults to ="hand-edit"= when
nothing binds it, so every audit record written by apply would be
misattributed. That attribution matters *more* after the cutover, not
less, since apply becomes the only writer of live state.

*Also to decide, not just "delete or keep":* what happens to the
=:SESSIONS:= data already sitting in TODO.org and DONE.org headings.
Retiring the mechanism does not retroactively delete history — the
options are leaving it in place as inert historical record, converting
it to =:LOGBOOK:= guidepost annotations, or dropping it. Probably the
first, but it is a separate question from whether anything new gets
written.


*Contradiction with the epic's plan, found 2026-08-11.* This heading
records the =:SESSIONS:= question as deliberately deferred and =MAYBE=.
=plan-this-task-clever-cupcake.md='s decision 5 records the opposite —
retirement *confirmed with the user* on 2026-08-06, with the further
consequence that =2b0988ed= becomes moot and should be CANCELLED.

One of the two is wrong, and it is not a bookkeeping detail: it decides
whether a drawer, its parser, its consolidator and their tests get
deleted. This heading is the later document and was written after a day
of implementation, which argues for it — but the plan claims explicit
user confirmation, which is stronger evidence than recency. Resolve it
before either is acted on.

*Reframed 2026-08-11, and then answered.* The arguments recorded above —
=org_log_background_plan='s lone remaining write, and CLAUDE.md's
presentation of the two drawers as co-equal — are the weak ones. The
question that actually decides this heading is: *what is a drained queue
file?*

After the cutover, =:LOGBOOK:= holds only the human-confirmed residue —
the intervals someone approved at review. Every pause and resume the
human declined to turn into an interval exists *only* in the raw queue
files. But CLAUDE.md's stated job for =:SESSIONS:= is the full wall-clock
arc *including the gaps*. So:
- if queue files are disposable, the arc-including-gaps has no durable
  home, and something has to keep it;
- if queue files are permanent, the =.jsonl= does that job strictly
  better than the drawer ever did, carrying =session_id=, =agent_id=,
  =agent_type= and =source= — none of which =:SESSIONS:= records.

The two plans disagreed on exactly this: the epic plan contemplated
deleting fully-applied files, while =event-queue-format.md= argued
deletion "loses history the review UI may still want."

*The user's answer, 2026-08-11:* neither — archive drained files rather
than deleting them, and make ingestion idempotent so an archived file
can be restored and reprocessed after a logic change or a corruption.
That makes queue files permanent by default without committing to never
tidying the live directory, and it is tracked as
[[id:78f485a8-e808-43ce-af5f-277a5c97c471][78f485a8]].

*Consequence for this heading: retire the drawer.* With the raw history
durable and restorable, =:SESSIONS:= has no job left that the queue does
not do better. What remains is the narrower question this heading
already poses correctly — what happens to the =:SESSIONS:= data already
sitting in TODO.org and DONE.org — where leaving it in place as inert
legacy history is still the obvious answer.

*Decided and half executed 2026-08-11.* The user's call, and it went
further than either option this heading had contemplated: delete the
existing drawers *entirely* rather than leaving them as inert legacy
history, on the grounds that they are distracting, that their retrospective
information content is low, and that post-cutover history will be much more
informative. The =:BLOCKER:= on
[[id:feba67eb-35b3-48bd-a892-8ecd47ca52e0][feba67eb]] is therefore
discharged by decision rather than by the cutover landing.

*The data was worth measuring before deleting it.* 49 drawers, 105 entry
lines across TODO.org and DONE.org. 91 were exactly the day-collapsed
=Paused=/=Resumed= noise described — already lossy, since consolidation
had long since reduced each day to one min-to-max pair. The other 14 were
a different kind of record and were surfaced explicitly before anything
was removed: 9 =Background-planned [ts] (session ID)= entries, the only
trace of which session background-planned a heading and when, and 5
=Paused ... (recovered)= markers from crash repair. The user chose to
delete those too — "entirely means entirely" — knowing what they were.
Everything is recoverable from git: both files were committed and pushed
before the deletion.

*Done:*
- All three =--log-session-event= call sites removed (clock-in, clock-out,
  session-resume), and the function itself deleted. Nothing writes a
  =:SESSIONS:= line by any path.
- =org_log_background_plan= no longer records its =session-id=. The
  parameter stays in the signature, so the MCP schema and callers are
  unaffected and there is an obvious place to reattach attribution as a
  queued event.
- All 49 drawers deleted, via =org-element-map= over real =drawer=
  elements rather than a text regex, so a =":SESSIONS:"= mentioned in
  prose was never at risk. Verified: zero drawers, zero orphaned entry
  lines, both files still parse, heading counts unchanged (69 and 23).
- Tests: two whose entire subject was the drawer deleted; three rewritten
  to assert what the wrapper actually promises (the clock closes, the
  clock lands back on the right heading) plus the drawer's *absence*, so
  its return would be a failure rather than a silent regression.
- CLAUDE.md and the org skill updated. The "cosmetic quirk" about
  =:SESSIONS:=/=:LOGBOOK:= ordering is resolved outright: with one drawer
  there is nothing to order.

*Remaining, and the reason this heading is still DOING:* the machinery is
now dead code that parses a format nothing writes —
=--parse-session-lines=, =--format-session-line=,
=--consolidate-sessions-text=, =consolidate-history='s =:SESSIONS:= half,
the recovery scan for an unclosed =Resumed=, and =close-open-interval='s
=:SESSIONS:= branch, plus their tests. It is inert rather than harmful,
which is why stopping here is a coherent state, but leaving it misleads
the next reader exactly as CLAUDE.md warns.

*Worth knowing before that cleanup starts:* =consolidate-history= may have
nothing left to do at all. [[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]]
removed its rounding and merging, leaving the =:LOGBOOK:= half a
normaliser that returns its input; removing the =:SESSIONS:= half removes
the only transformation it still performs. That makes it a candidate for
deletion outright rather than trimming — which is what this heading
predicted, and which reaches into
[[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]]'s territory, since
=close-open-interval= is one of its two callers.

*Machinery removed 2026-08-11; this heading is now fully executed.*
=--parse-session-lines=, =--format-session-line= and
=--consolidate-sessions-text= are deleted. =consolidate-history= lost its
=:SESSIONS:= half, =close-open-interval= lost both its
"Paused ... (recovered)" write *and* its =consolidate-history= call, and
the stale-interval scan no longer looks for an unclosed =Resumed= —
=--entry-open-interval= and =find-stale-open-intervals= dropped
=:sessions-open= throughout. Audited afterwards: not one ="SESSIONS"=
string literal remains anywhere in =config.el=; every surviving mention is
prose explaining the retirement.

*Two consequential decisions taken inside this cleanup, both arguable:*
- *=consolidate-history= was kept, not deleted,* though it is now close
  to a no-op — [[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]] took
  its rounding and merging, and this took its second drawer, leaving only
  ordering. Kept because
  [[id:af7d3687-6d1e-4480-a9e2-df772785713f][af7d3687]] is precisely a
  change to this function; deleting it now would mean recreating it. It
  has no automatic caller, which is the point.
- *=close-open-interval= no longer consolidates.* Rewriting a whole
  drawer as a side effect of repairing one line is the shape behind both
  [[id:ba8249c1-28cd-4ff1-918b-4b8439345d9a][ba8249c1]] and =b74e0f19=,
  and consolidation had nothing left to contribute there. This reaches
  into [[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]]'s territory,
  since =close-open-interval= was =consolidate-history='s only remaining
  caller — that subsystem's fate is now a cleaner question than it was.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 163/163 (three tests deleted as their whole
subject was the drawer, four rewritten to assert the drawer's *absence*
so its return fails rather than passing silently). Live-reloaded, with
the three deleted functions explicitly =fmakunbound= — a =load-file=
re-evaluates what is in a file but does not unbind what was removed from
it, so the running image would otherwise have kept them.

*One incident worth recording, because it proves the point.* Immediately
after deleting all 49 drawers I clocked out, and a drawer came back — on
*this heading*. The live Emacs was still running the pre-edit writer,
because =config.el= had been changed on disk but never reloaded. Caught
by the post-deletion count, not by foresight; the reload precondition
should have been stated before the check rather than after it.


*** DONE Reconcile or retire the now-likely-superseded clock/session-ownership plans and infrastructure :code:
:PROPERTIES:

*Reconciled 2026-08-14. All six are settled; four had already settled

*Four =CANCELLED= on 2026-08-12, each with a note giving its reason:*

as this heading asked. The fourth points at =af7d3687= instead, which is

*Which makes the parenthetical prediction above exactly wrong, and it is
worth keeping rather than quietly correcting.* =2b0988ed= was named as

unrelated to the refactor, while =53b0047d= — unmentioned in that aside

outlive a refactor from their subject matter did not work here.

*Two survive, both confirmed rather than assumed:*
- =53b0047d= — promoted =MAYBE= -> =TODO=. Not superseded: observed live

*The infrastructure half, which the title names and the body above never
